Anderson stochastic quantization equation
Hugo Eulry, Antoine Mouzard, and Tristan Robert

Abstract
. We study the parabolic defocusing stochastic quantization equation with both mutliplicative spatial white noise and an independant space-time white noise forcing, on compact surfaces, with polynomial nonlinearity. After renormalizing the nonlinearity, we construct the random Gibbs measure as an absolutely continuous measure with respect to the law of the Anderson Gaussian Free Field for fixed realization of the spatial white noise. Then, when the initial data is distributed according to the Gibbs measure, we prove almost sure global well-posedness for the dynamics and invariance of the Gibbs measure.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
A singular SPDE in a singular random environment

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a closed Riemannian surface,
2
-dimensional, compact and boundaryless. We consider the parabolic defocusing stochastic quantization equation

(1.1)

\displaystyle{}\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u+\mathcal{H}u+F^{\prime}(u)=\sqrt{2}\zeta,\\
u(0)=u_{0},\end{cases}

where

(1.2)

\displaystyle F(X)=\sum_{k=0}^{2m}a_{k}X^{k}

is a polynomial of even degree
2m
,
\zeta
is a space-time white noise on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{M}
, and
\mathcal{H}
denotes the Anderson Hamiltonian operator. The latter is a rough and random perturbation of the Laplace-Beltrami operator:

(1.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\approx-\Delta+\xi,

where
\xi
is a spatial white noise on
\mathcal{M}
, independent of
\zeta
.

This study finds its place among the many other ones dealing with invariant measures for singular stochastic PDEs. If we were to get rid of the spatial white noise and consider
-\Delta
instead of
\mathcal{H}
, then (
1.1
) corresponds to the usual stochastic quantization equation of the
P(\Phi_{2})
model, for which the analogue of the Gibbs measure (
1.4
) was constructed as part of the development of the Euclidean Quantum Field Theory in the 60s and 70s (see e.g.
[
50
]
and references therein), while the corresponding dynamics was first studied in
[
14
]
; see also
[
23
,
35
,
53
]
, among others. Other dynamics leaving this measure invariant were also studied: wave equations
[
26
,
27
,
39
,
43
,
44
]
, Schrödinger equations
[
6
,
9
,
20
,
42
,
54
]
, and complex Ginzburg-Landau equations
[
16
,
48
,
52
]
, to name but a few.

Without the stochastic forcing term
\zeta
in (
1.1
) and with deterministic initial data, the equation becomes the Parabolic Anderson Model with polynomial nonlinearity, amenable to both the paracontrolled calculus approach
[
24
]
or the approach using regularity structures
[
28
]
. Recent progress on the understanding of the Anderson Hamiltonian
[
1
,
24
,
25
,
31
,
29
,
34
]
opened the door to the study of dynamics in singular environment with regular enough deterministic data, such as the Anderson heat equation
[
24
,
30
]
, or Anderson wave and Schrödinger equations
[
17
,
18
,
19
,
25
,
36
,
55
,
56
,
57
]
. In this paper, we go one step further and study a singular stochastic PDE in a singular random environment, namely the Anderson heat equation with polynomial nonlinearity and both rough deterministic or random initial data, and singular stochastic source term (
1.1
). A similar approach was recently considered by Barashkov, De Vecchi, and Zachhuber
[
3
]
, who studied the problem of invariance of the Gibbs measure for the cubic non-linear Anderson wave equation.

1.2.
Main results and sketch of the proof

Because
\xi
in (
1.3
) is almost surely of regularity
1
1

1

\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}
denotes the Hölder space of regularity
\sigma
; see Definition
A.1
below.

\mathcal{C}^{-1-\kappa}
for any
\kappa>0
, the very definition of
\mathcal{H}
is tricky as one needs to go through a renormalization procedure to make sense of it as an unbounded
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
operator. Its precise definition is given in Section
4
, see for instance
[
1
,
25
,
31
,
34
]
and references therein for the whole story, the most important point being that
\mathcal{H}
can be defined as an unbounded positive self-adjoint operator with dense domain and compact resolvent. In this work, we then construct a Gibbs type measure for (
1.1
) and prove local and almost sure global well-posedness and invariance of this measure.

The noiseless version of (
1.1
) has indeed a gradient flow structure

\partial_{t}u+\nabla_{u}\mathcal{E}(u)=0,

with energy

\mathcal{E}(u):=\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}u\mathcal{H}u+F(u)\Big]\,dx.

Following the finite dimensional mantra, this yields a formal Gibbs measure for (
1.1
) under the form

d\rho(u)=\mathcal{Z}^{-1}e^{-\mathcal{E}(u)}du,

or more precisely

(1.4)

\displaystyle{}d\rho(u)=\mathcal{Z}^{-1}e^{-\int_{\mathcal{M}}\big[\frac{1}{2}u\mathcal{H}u+F(u)\big]dx}du.

As it is, (
1.4
) is only a formal expression and several problems arise when we try to make sense of it. First of all,
du
supposedly refers to an infinite dimensional Lebesgue measure, which has no proper definition. The usual trick is to consider instead the quadratic part of the density together with
du
so that it defines a Gaussian measure

d\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(u):=\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{-1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}}u\mathcal{H}u\,dx}du,

see Subsection
4.2
for the details on
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
. It is defined as the centered Gaussian measure on
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
with covariance operator
\mathcal{H}^{-1}
. However, since
\mathcal{H}^{-1}
is not trace class on
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
,
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
will rather be supported on strictly larger spaces, namely
H^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
for any
\varepsilon>0
. Another equivalent definition of
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
would be to match the law of the Gaussian Free Field (GFF) associated to
\mathcal{H}
, that is the random series

(1.5)

\displaystyle{}u^{\omega}(x):=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{\gamma_{n}(\omega)}{\sqrt{\lambda_{n}}}\varphi_{n}(x)

where
\gamma_{n}
are i.i.d. standard Gaussian random variables,
\lambda_{n}
the eigenvalues for
\mathcal{H}
and
\varphi_{n}
the corresponding
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
-orthonormal basis of eigenfunctions. This random series can be shown to almost surely converge in
H^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
for positive
\varepsilon
but not in
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
. This poses another difficulty in that typical elements
u
in the support of
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
would then be distributions, making the non-linear term
F(u)
ill-defined as
u\notin L^{2}(\mathcal{M})

\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
-almost surely. This can be handled by performing a Wick renormalization of the non-linear term
F(u)
that will be described in Subsection
2.2
. In short, if

(1.6)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{N}:=\psi(N^{-2}\Delta)

is a Schwartz multiplier, then

\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\left[|\mathbf{P}_{N}u|^{2}(x)\right]=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}|\mathbf{P}_{N}\varphi_{n}|^{2}(x)\rightarrow+\infty

as
N
goes to
+\infty
. This is due to the fact that the Green’s function of
\mathcal{H}
on the diagonal has a logarithmic divergence, see (
4.6
) for a precise statement. Thus, Wick reordering replaces monomials
X^{k}
by Hermite polynomials
H_{k}(X,\sigma^{2})
with variance
\sigma^{2}
. Writing

(1.7)

\displaystyle\sigma_{N}^{2}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\left[|\mathbf{P}_{N}u|^{2}(x)\right],

we then define the corresponding renormalized monomial

(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond k}:=H_{k}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u,\sigma_{N}^{2})

and extend the definition to polynomials as in (
1.2
) by linearity:

(1.8)

\displaystyle F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)=\sum_{k=0}^{2m}a_{k}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond k}=\sum_{k=0}^{2m}a_{k}H_{k}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u,\sigma_{N}^{2}).

The key point is that we get convergence of the renormalized non-linearity as
N\to+\infty
, that is

\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx\to\int_{\mathcal{M}}F^{\diamond}(u)\,dx

in
L^{p}(d\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
for any
p\geq 1
; see Proposition
2.6
below. Definition and convergence properties of Hermite polynomials will be explained in Subsection
2.2
. From now on,
\sigma_{N}
is fixed to be as above and all the renormalized powers will be constructed with respect to
\sigma_{N}
. After this regularization and renormalization, we are left with an approximate Gibbs measure

(1.9)

\displaystyle{}d\rho_{N}(u):=\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{-1}e^{-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx}\,d\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(u).

Recall that we work with a defocusing non-linearity, thus
\rho_{N}
is well-defined for any fixed
N
. Our first result is the following, where we construct the Gibbs measure (
1.4
) as the limit of the sequence
(\rho_{N})_{N}
.

Theorem 1.1
.

The sequence
(\rho_{N})_{N}
converges in total variation to a measure
\rho
that is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
.

Detailed proof will be given in Subsection
2.3
but the core idea, once convergence of the renormalized polynomials has been established, is to use Boué-Dupuis’ formula as introduced in
[
4
]
and
[
5
]
to get uniform boundedness of the density.

Now that a candidate Gibbs measure has been constructed, we have to build the corresponding dynamics and check that the measure stays invariant under the flow. A natural way to do so is to truncate the equation

(1.10)

\displaystyle{}\partial_{t}u_{N}+\mathcal{H}u_{N}+\mathbf{P}_{N}f^{\diamond}_{N}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u_{N})=\sqrt{2}\zeta

where
f_{n}^{\diamond}=(F_{N}^{\diamond})^{\prime}
. Local well-posedness can be proved using Da Prato Debussche trick, from there, it is only a matter of proving that solutions exist globally for some well chosen subset of initial data and that we can pass
N
to the limit. We first show local-well posedness and convergence of the solution to (
1.10
) for deterministic initial data.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
0<\epsilon<\frac{1}{2m-1}
,
\varepsilon<\sigma<1
, and
q>1
be such that
\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}q^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2m-1}
. Then
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, for any
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
and
T_{0}>0
, there exists
T\in(0;T_{0}\wedge 1]
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, (
1.10
) admits a solution
u_{N}\in C([0;T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
, unique in the affine space

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t)
lives.

Our next result deals with globalization of the solution in the case where the initial data is random and distributed by the Gibbs measure
\rho
constructed in Theorem
1.1
, and also proves that the law of the solution is invariant and given by
\rho
.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
\varepsilon
and
\sigma
be as in Theorem
1.2
. If
u_{0}
is distributed according to
\rho
, then almost surely, for any
T>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the truncated equation (
1.10
) admits a unique solution in
\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
. Moreover
u_{N}
converges in
C([0;T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
to the unique solution
u\in\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
to (
1.11
), and the law of
u(t)
does not depend on
t
and is given by
\rho
.

As mentionned above,
\zeta
denotes a space-time white noise on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{M}
that is independent of
\mathcal{H}
. In other words,
\zeta
is a centered Gaussian process indexed by
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{M})
functions and with covariance given by

\mathbb{E}\left[\zeta(\varphi)\zeta(\psi)\right]=\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{M})},

which is why
\zeta
is said to have
delta
correlations since formaly the pointwise correlations are
\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(t,x)\zeta(s,y)\big]=\delta_{0}(x-y)\delta_{0}(t-s)
. Note that given any
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
orthonormal basis
(\varphi_{n})_{n}
, we have the decomposition

\zeta(t)dt=\sum_{n\geq 0}dB_{n}(t)\varphi_{n}

where
B_{n}(t):=\langle\zeta,\mathds{1}_{[0,t]}\varphi_{n}\rangle
are i.i.d. standard Brownian motion on
\mathbb{R}
. Since
\mathcal{H}
itself has an
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
-orthonormal basis of eigenfunctions, the aforementioned expansion of
\zeta
can be done using these eigenfunctions.

Remark 1.4
.

\mathcal{H}
being a random operator for which we fix a realisation
\mathcal{H}^{\omega^{\prime}}
, then
\zeta
would write

\zeta^{\omega}(t)dt=\sum_{n\geq 0}dB_{n}^{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}(t)\varphi_{n}^{\omega^{\prime}}

for a realisation
\omega
. Note that
\zeta
itself does not depend on
\omega^{\prime}
while it coefficients
B_{n}
do depend on both
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
. In the following, we fix a realisation
\omega^{\prime}
so that
\mathcal{H}^{\omega^{\prime}}
is defined once and for all. In other words, all the probabilistic considerations have to be taken conditionally to
\mathcal{H}
. In particular, while we strongly believe that the double layer of randomness would be worth studying, it is out of the scope of this paper and we provide an approach that only relies on properties derived from the construction of
\mathcal{H}
. Section
4
will recall all the material needed here.

The main difficulty in the solution theory of the renormalized equation (
1.10
) is then the operator
\mathcal{H}
itself. On the one hand, the formal expression (
1.3
) can be made rigorous, namely
\mathcal{H}
can be written (through conjugation with the so-called
\Gamma
map, see (
4.2
) below) as a perturbation of
-\Delta
. On the other hand, the perturbative terms have very rough coefficients, so that one has to carefully check that the classical analytic toolbox for the Laplace-Beltrami operator used in the context of more standard singular stochastic PDEs can be adapted to our setting despite those rough coefficients. In particular, we only dispose of a Schauder estimate with limited range of exponents (see (
4.3
) below), and we have to use the precise mapping properties of the perturbative terms in
\mathcal{H}
to show that the Green’s function for
\mathcal{H}
has the same singularities as the one for
-\Delta
, which is crucial in building the Wick powers of the stochastic convolution to any degree; see Corollary
4.5
and Proposition
2.4
.

We conclude this introduction with some further remarks.

Remark 1.5
.

The solution constructed in Theorem
1.2
for arbitrary initial data in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
is only local in time, while using the Gibbs measure we could globalize the solution only for
\rho
-almost every initial data in Theorem
1.3
. The question of deterministic global well-posedness and ergodicity of the dynamics is an interesting open question, and will be investigated in a follow-up work.

Remark 1.6
.

We made sense of the formal equation (
1.1
) through a limiting procedure involving the equation with regularized nonlinearity (
1.10
). In the field of singular stochastic PDEs, it is more common to instead regularize the noise and replace
\zeta
by some smooth approximation
\mathbf{P}_{N}\zeta
and study the convergence of the corresponding solutions
u_{N}
to the renormalized equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{N}+\mathcal{H}u_{N}+f_{N}^{\diamond}(u_{N})=\sqrt{2}\mathbf{P}_{N}\zeta

where now the renormalized nonlinearity
f_{N}^{\diamond}
is not truncated by the operator
\mathbf{P}_{N}
contrary to (
1.10
).
However, (
1.10
) is better-suited for our purpose since (i) we want
in fine
to prove the invariance of the Gibbs measure, which we only know how to do by using a finite-dimensional approximation of the dynamics which has itself a finite-dimensional approximation of the Gibbs measure as invariant measure; and (ii) since
\mathcal{H}
and
-\Delta
do not commute, and since the regularizing operator
\mathbf{P}_{N}
is a Schwartz multiplier based on
-\Delta
, it is not as straightforward to study the stochastic convolution
\widetilde{\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}}(t):=\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\mathbf{P}_{N}\zeta(ds)
and its Wick powers to get the analogue of Proposition
2.4
.

Remark 1.7
.

In (
1.1
),
\mathcal{H}
is a Schrödinger operator with rough random electric potential
\xi
. The same arguments as those in the present paper can also deal with (
1.1
) where (
1.3
) is replaced by
(i\nabla+A)^{2}
, where now
A
is a rough and random magnetic potential. The construction of the corresponding magnetic Anderson operator when the magnetic field
B=\nabla\wedge A
is a spatial white noise, namely
\nabla\wedge A=\xi
, has been carried out in
[
33
]
. In this case, (
1.1
) can be interpreted as the stochastic quantization equation for the marginal of the
P(\Phi_{2})
-Yang-Mills-Higgs measure. Indeed, the abelian Yang-Mill-Higgs measure is the Gibbs type measure formally written as

\displaystyle``d\rho=\mathcal{Z}^{-\mathcal{E}(A,\Phi)}dAd\Phi",

where the abelian
P(\Phi_{2})
-Yang-Mills-Higgs action is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(A,\Phi)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[B^{2}+|(i\nabla+A)\Phi|^{2}+F(\Phi)\Big]dx;

see e.g.
[
49
]
.
Upon fixing a global gauge to have a one-to-one correspondence between
A
and
B
, we can then rewrite the measure similarly as in (
1.4
) by

\displaystyle``d\rho=\mathcal{Z}^{-1}e^{-\int_{\mathcal{M}}\big[\frac{1}{2}\Phi\mathcal{H}_{B}\Phi+F(\Phi)\big]dx}d\Phi d\mu(B),

where
\mathcal{H}_{B}=(i\nabla+A)^{2}
and

\displaystyle d\mu(B)=\mathcal{Z}^{-1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}}B^{2}dx}dB

is the white noise measure. Given a realization of
B
, the measure
d\rho_{B}=\mathcal{Z}^{-1}e^{-\int_{\mathcal{M}}\big[\frac{1}{2}\Phi\mathcal{H}_{B}\Phi+F(\Phi)\big]dx}d\Phi
is then the analogue of the measure (
1.4
), with the white noise electric potential
\xi
replaced by the white noise magnetic field
B
, and one can study its invariance under a parabolic singular stochastic dynamics similar to (
1.1
).

Remark 1.8
.

Since for fixed time, the stochastic convolution for the Anderson stochastic heat equation and that of the Anderson stochastic damped wave equation

(1.12)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}u+\mathcal{H}u+\partial_{t}u=\sqrt{2}\zeta

share the same covariance function (see e.g.
[
41
]
), given by the Green’s function of
\mathcal{H}
, then Proposition
2.4
also holds for the Wick powers of the solution to (
1.12
). Together with the same nonlinear analysis in
L^{2}
-based Sobolev spaces
3
3

3

We can simply use that
\mathcal{D}^{-\varepsilon}=H^{-\varepsilon}
and
\mathcal{D}^{1-\varepsilon}=H^{1-\varepsilon}
, and that the propagator
\frac{\sin(t\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})}{\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}}
maps
\mathcal{D}^{-\varepsilon}
to
\mathcal{D}^{1-\varepsilon}
from the functional calculus.
as e.g. in
[
39
]
, this shows that the analogue of Theorem
1.3
also holds true for the stochastic damped Anderson nonlinear wave equation with any polynomial nonlinearity

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}u+\mathcal{H}u+\partial_{t}u+f^{\diamond}(u)=\sqrt{2}\zeta.

Plan of the paper

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
we construct the renormalized powers of the stochastic objects in play. We also use Boué-Dupuis formula to construct the Gibbs measure as the limit in total variation of its approximations (Theorem
1.1
). In Section
3
, we first prove local well-posedness for deterministic initial data in
\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}(\mathcal{M})
, that is Theorem
1.2
. Then we prove invariance of the measure as well as global well-posedness for random initial data distributed according to the Gibbs measure, that is Theorem
1.3
. Section
4
is devoted to recalling the construction of the Anderson operator
\mathcal{H}
and some of its properties, together with a study of its Green’s function and of its Schartz multipliers. Finally, in Appendix
A
, we gather the analytic toolbox that we need in analyzing the Wick powers and in the nonlinear estimates for our well-posedness theory.

2.
Construction of the stochastic objects

In this section we provide the probabilistic framework to make sense of the renormalization procedure involved in the definition of
\rho_{N}
in (
1.9
) and also prove Theorem
1.1
.

2.1.
Algebraic setting and Wick powers

We first of all recall the definition of Hermite polynomials and the corresponding algebraic rules, refering to
[
37
]
or
[
15
]
for more insights on the whole story. We denote by
H_{k}
the
k
-th Hermite polynomial defined by the generating series

e^{tx-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}}=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{t^{k}}{k!}H_{k}(x)

for
x,t\in\mathbb{R}
. From this expansion, we easily get the recursion formula

H_{k}^{\prime}=kH_{k-1}

for any positive integer
k
. Such polynomials are heavily related to Gaussian variables in the sense of the following lemma, see
[
37
, Lemma 1.1.1]
for a proof.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
X,Y
be real standard Gaussian random variables. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[H_{k}(X)H_{l}(Y)\right]=k!\delta_{k,l}\mathbb{E}[XY]^{k}.

Note that we have an extra
k!
factor here, this is due to our normalization of Hermite polynomials so that they have leading coefficient 1 as opposed to
\frac{1}{k!}
in
[
37
]
. We also get the corresponding binomial formula.

Lemma 2.2
.

For
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}

H_{k}(x+y)=\sum_{p=0}^{k}\binom{k}{p}x^{k-p}H_{p}(y).

Proof.

It is a direct consequence of the definition of
H_{k}
by the generating series and the following expansion for
x,y,t\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{t^{k}}{k!}H_{k}(x+y)

\displaystyle=e^{(x+y)t-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{xt}e^{yt-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{t^{n}}{n!}x^{n}\sum_{p\geq 0}\frac{t^{p}}{p!}H_{p}(y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=n+p}\frac{t^{k}}{k!}\frac{k!}{p!n!}x^{n}H_{p}(y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{t^{k}}{k!}\sum_{p=0}^{k}\binom{k}{p}x^{k-p}H_{p}(y).

∎

We then define the Hermite polynomials with variance
\sigma^{2}>0
as

H_{k}(x,\sigma^{2}):=\sigma^{k}H_{k}(\sigma^{-1}x),

which allows to transfer the algebraic rules (
2.1
) and (
2.2
) to Gaussian random variables with non-unit variance. In the following, for a real centered Gaussian random variable
X
, we will use the shorthand notation called
Wick power
of
X

X^{\diamond k}:=H_{k}(X,\sigma^{2}),

where
\sigma^{2}=\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]
. We end this subsection with the Wiener chaos estimate, see for example
[
50
, Theorem I.22]
.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
g=(g_{n})
be a sequence of i.i.d. real standard Gaussian random variables on some probability space
\Omega
and
(P_{j})
be a family of polynomials in
g
with degree at most
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then for
p\geq 2

\Big\|\sum_{j\geq 0}P_{j}(g)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq(p-1)^{\frac{k}{2}}\Big\|\sum_{j\geq 0}P_{j}(g)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

2.2.
Renormalization

As progressing through the construction of the Gibbs measure (
1.4
), we will encounter truncated stochastic objects for which we would like to take the limit in the truncation parameter. This will either be for the non-linearity in the energy

\int_{\mathcal{M}}F^{\diamond}_{N}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx,

where
u
is distributed according to the GFF
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
and
F_{N}^{\diamond}
defined in (
1.8
), or for the stochastic convolution involved in the construction of the renormalized dynamic

(2.1)

\displaystyle{}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t):=\sqrt{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\zeta(ds)

and its mollification

\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}(t):=\sqrt{2}\mathbf{P}_{N}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\zeta(ds).

Proposition 2.4
.

Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
, for any
T>0
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
p\geq q\geq 1
the sequence
(\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\diamond k}_{N})_{N}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{q}([0,T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})))
that also converges almost surely in
L^{q}([0,T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
. Moreover
t\mapsto\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}(t)
and the limiting process
t\mapsto\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t)
are almost surely in
C([0,T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
and we have the following tail estimates and deviation bounds:

(2.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}_{x}^{-\varepsilon}}>R\right)\leq Ce^{-cR^{\frac{2}{k}}T^{-\frac{2}{qk}}},

and

(2.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}-\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\diamond k}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}_{x}^{-\varepsilon}}>N^{-\kappa}R\right)\leq Ce^{-cR^{\frac{2}{k}}T^{-\frac{2}{qk}}},

for some constant
c,C>0
, and exponent
\kappa>0
that do not depend on
T
,
R
,
p
, or
N
.

Proof.

As a starter, we prove that
(\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\diamond k}_{N}(t))_{N}
is bounded in
L^{p}(\Omega;\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\geq 0
.
Using Itô’s isometry and expanding
\zeta
in the eigenbasis
(\varphi_{n})
of
\mathcal{H}
, we get for
t\geq 0
,
x,y\in\mathcal{M}
and
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N_{1}}(t,x)\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N_{2}}(t,y)\right]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}^{x}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}^{y})\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\zeta^{x}(ds)e^{-(t-\tau)\mathcal{H}}\zeta^{y}(d\tau)\right](x,y)

\displaystyle=2(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}^{x}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}^{y})\sum_{n,m\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda_{n}}\varphi_{n}^{x}dB_{n}(s)e^{-(t-\tau)\lambda_{m}}\varphi_{m}^{y}dB_{m}(\tau)\right](x,y)

\displaystyle=2(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}^{x}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}^{y})\left(\sum_{n\geq 0}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-2(t-s)\lambda_{n}}\varphi_{n}^{x}\varphi_{n}^{y}\,ds\right)(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\varphi_{n})(x)(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\varphi_{n})(y)}{\lambda_{n}}

\displaystyle=G^{\mathcal{H}}_{N_{1},N_{2}}(x,y),

where
G^{\mathcal{H}}_{N_{1},N_{2}}(x,y)=(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}^{x}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}^{y})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)
and
G^{\mathcal{H}}
is the Green function of
\mathcal{H}
. Since the covariance above does not depend on time, we drop the
t
dependency as it does not matter in the estimates. Combining the Sobolev inequality (Lemma
A.3
(i) and (ii)) with the Wiener chaos estimate (
2.3
), we get for
\sigma\in(0,1)
small enough depending on
\varepsilon
and
p
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{W^{-\sigma,p}}^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left((1-\Delta)^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}(\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k})(x)\right)^{p}\right]\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left((1-\Delta)^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}(\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k})(x)\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx

\displaystyle=(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}G_{\sigma}(x,y)G_{\sigma}(x,z)\mathbb{E}\left[\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}(z)\right]\,dy\,dz\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx,

where
G_{\sigma}
is the Green function of
(1-\Delta)^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}
.
From there, we use the previous computation and the algebraic rule (
2.1
) to get rid of the expectation so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\right]\lesssim(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}G_{\sigma}(x,y)G_{\sigma}(x,z)G_{N}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}\,dy\,dz\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx.

Recall from (
A.12
) and (
4.8
) that

G_{\sigma}(x,y)\lesssim\textbf{d}(x,y)^{\sigma-2}

and

\left|G_{N}^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(\textbf{d}(x,y)+N^{-1}\big)\right|\leq C.

Thus once we replace inside the integral it yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}\textbf{d}(x,y)^{\sigma-2}\textbf{d}(x,z)^{\sigma-2}(1+|\log(\textbf{d}(y,z)+N^{-1})|^{k})\,dy\,dz\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx.

Note that, since we integrate symmetric functions of
(y,z)\in\mathcal{M}^{2}
, we can restrict the integral to
\textbf{d}(x,y)\geq\textbf{d}(x,z)
so that, using the triangle inequality on
\textbf{d}(y,z)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}

\displaystyle+(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\int_{\textbf{d}(x,y)\geq\textbf{d}(x,z)}\textbf{d}(x,y)^{\sigma-2}\textbf{d}(x,z)^{\sigma-2}|\log(\textbf{d}(x,y))|^{k}\,dy\,dz\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx.

Since
|\log(\textbf{d}(x,y))|^{k}\lesssim_{k}\textbf{d}(x,y)^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}
, we end up with

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\right]\lesssim(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}+(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\int_{\textbf{d}(x,y)\geq\textbf{d}(x,z)}\textbf{d}(x,y)^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-2}\textbf{d}(x,z)^{\sigma-2}\,dy\,dz\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx,

where the inner integral on the right-hand side is bounded uniformly in
x\in\mathcal{M}
as
\mathcal{M}
is 2-dimensional and compact. Using next Minkowski’s integral inequality, we get

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{k}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}_{x}}^{p}\right]\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{k}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}_{x}}^{p}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\|_{L^{q}_{T}}^{p}\lesssim(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}T^{\frac{p}{q}}.

This gives the uniform boundedness of
\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\diamond k}_{N}
in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{q}([0,T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})))
for any
q\geq 1
. Chebychev’s inequality and optimizing in
p
then ensures that the tail estimate (
2.2
) holds true:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N}^{\diamond k}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}_{x}}>R\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}R^{-p}T^{\frac{p}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-cR^{\frac{2}{k}}T^{-\frac{2}{qk}}}

for some constants
c,C
that do not depend on

,
R
,
p
, or
N
.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N_{2}}^{\diamond k}(y)\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{N_{2}}^{\diamond k}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=G_{N_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}-G_{N_{1}N_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}-G_{N_{2}N_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}+G_{N_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}.

Using successively the mean value inequality, estimate (
4.5
), and again the comparison between logarithm and monomials, we get for any
0<\delta\ll 1
:

\displaystyle|G_{N_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}-G_{N_{1}N_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}|

\displaystyle\lesssim|G_{N_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)-G_{N_{1}N_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)|(1+\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-\frac{\sigma}{6}})

\displaystyle\lesssim((|\log(\textbf{d}(y,z)+N_{1}^{-1})|\vee 1)\wedge(N_{1}^{\delta-1}\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-1}))(1+\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-\frac{\sigma}{6}}).

In the case
\mathbf{d}(x,y)\leq N_{1}^{-1}
, we have by interpolation and the comparison between logarithm and monomials that

\displaystyle((|\log(\textbf{d}(y,z)+N_{1}^{-1})|\vee 1)\wedge(N_{1}^{\delta-1}\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-1}))

\displaystyle\lesssim\log N_{1}\wedge(N_{1}^{\delta-1}\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-1}))

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta,\theta}N_{1}^{(\frac{\delta}{2}-1)\theta}\mathbf{d}(y,z)^{-\theta}

for any
\theta\in(0;1)
, uniformly in
N_{1}
, while in the case
\mathbf{d}(x,y)\geq N_{1}^{-1}
we get

\displaystyle((|\log(\textbf{d}(y,z)+N_{1}^{-1})|\vee 1)\wedge(N_{1}^{\delta-1}\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-1}))

\displaystyle\lesssim\log\mathbf{d}(y,z)^{-1}\wedge(N_{1}^{\delta-1}\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-1}))

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta,\theta}N^{(\delta-1)\theta}\mathbf{d}(y,z)^{-((1-\theta)\frac{\nu}{2}+\theta)}

for any
\theta\in(0;1)
and
0<\nu\ll 1
, uniformly in
N_{1}
. This finally gives

(2.4)

\displaystyle{}|G_{N_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}-G_{N_{1}N_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}|

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-2\kappa}\textbf{d}(y,z)^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}

for some
\kappa>0
and implicit constant that is uniform in
y,z
and
N_{1},N_{2}
. Similar computations as before yield that the whole inner integral on
\mathcal{M}^{2}
is bounded uniformly in
x\in\mathcal{M}
with

\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}G_{\sigma}(x,y)G_{\sigma}(x,z)\mathbb{E}\left[(\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

indeed is a Cauchy sequence in

, we denote by

the limiting process.

convergence ensures convergence in probability, we can use Chebychev’s inequality together with passing to the limit

and optimizing in

so that

\displaystyle\leq(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}N^{\kappa p}R^{-p}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\leq C(p-1)^{p\frac{k}{2}}R^{-p}T^{\frac{p}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-cR^{\frac{2}{k}}T^{-\frac{2}{qk}}}

for some constants
c,C>0
and the same exponent

defined above. Note that
c
,
C
and
\kappa
do not depend on
N,R
or
T
. This proves deviation estimate (
2.3
) as well as almost sure convergence in
L^{q}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
using Borel-Cantelli lemma.

\displaystyle\lesssim(p-1)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big(\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(x,y)G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(x,z)\mathbb{E}\big[(\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }

{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t+\delta,z)-\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t,z))\big]dydz\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}dx

\displaystyle=(p-1)^{\frac{p}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big(\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(x,y)G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(x,z)\big[(1-e^{-\delta\mathcal{H}})\otimes 1\big]G^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)dydz\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}dx.

Now, using Sobolev inequality,

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{\mathcal{M}}G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(x,y)\big[(1-e^{-\delta\mathcal{H}})\otimes 1\big]G^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)dy\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\big[(1-e^{-\delta\mathcal{H}})\otimes 1\big]G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,z)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}W^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\big[(1-e^{-\delta\mathcal{H}})\otimes 1\big]G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,z)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}\mathcal{D}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}_{x}},

with
\mathcal{D}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}
the Sobolev space associated to
\mathcal{H}
, see (
4.4
). Using the mean value inequality and the bound
|1-e^{-\delta\lambda_{n}}|^{2}\lesssim(\delta\lambda_{n})^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}
, for any fixed
z\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\big[(1-e^{-\delta\mathcal{H}})\otimes 1\big]G^{\mathcal{H}}(\cdot,z)\Big\|_{\mathcal{D}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{|1-e^{-\delta\lambda_{n}}|^{2}}{\lambda_{n}^{1+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}}\varphi_{n}(z)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\lambda_{n}^{-1-\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}\varphi_{n}(z)^{2}

\displaystyle\sim\delta^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}\|\delta_{z}\|_{\mathcal{D}^{-1-\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8}},

uniformly in
z\in\mathcal{M}
, where we used that
\|\delta_{z}\|_{\mathcal{D}^{-1-\frac{\varepsilon}{8}}}
is uniformly bounded in
z\in\mathcal{M}
, see the proof of
[
2
, Lemma 23]
. Since
G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}(x,z)\in L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{z}
, and since
\mathcal{M}
is compact, putting everything together we get
