uniformly in
R
,
p
, and
N,M
.

Similar results hold for
(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(0))^{\diamond k}
in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
.

Proof.

Repeating the arguments above in the proof of Proposition
2.4
, we have for fixed
t\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M_{1}}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t))^{\diamond k}-(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M_{2}}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t))^{\diamond k}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}(x,y)G_{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}(x,z)\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\left[G_{N,M_{1},M_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}-G_{N,M_{1},M_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}-G_{N,M_{2},M_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}+G_{N,M_{2},M_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(y,z)^{k}\right]\,dy\,dz\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,dx

for some
p\gg 1
depending on
\varepsilon
, and where

G_{N,M_{j},M_{\ell}}^{\mathcal{H}}=(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M_{j}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M_{\ell}})G^{\mathcal{H}}=(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})(\chi_{M_{j}}\otimes\chi_{M_{\ell}})G^{\mathcal{H}}.

Then using the mean value theorem and (
4.14
)-(
4.6
)-(
A.12
) we can estimate the inner integrals by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}-2}\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{4}-2}N^{c\delta}M_{1}^{-\delta}|\log(\mathbf{d}(y,z))|^{k-1}dydz\lesssim N^{c\delta}M_{1}^{-\delta}

for some small
\delta>0
. This is enough for (
2.6
) after finishing the proof as in that of Proposition
2.4
.

The estimate (
2.5
) follows similarly, using that we have a bound uniform in
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
from using only (
4.14
) and not (
4.6
).
∎

2.3.
Truncated Gibbs measure and convergence

We finally construct the Gibbs measure
\rho
as the limit in total variation of
(\rho_{N})_{N}
given by (
1.9
):

d\rho_{N}(u)=\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{-1}e^{-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx}\,d\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(u),

where
\mathcal{Z}_{N}
is the normalization constant

\mathcal{Z}_{N}:=\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\left[e^{-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx}\right].

First, we prove the convergence of the density functions.

Proposition 2.6
.

For any
p\geq 1
, the sequence
\left(\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx\right)_{N}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
, converging to a limit denoted by
\int_{\mathcal{M}}F^{\diamond}(u)\,dx\in L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
.

Proof.

Since
F
is a polynomial of degree
k=2m
, it is sufficient to treat the case where
F(X)=X^{k}
is a monomial. The proof follows the same lines as the one of Proposition (
2.4
). First, we have the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond k}\,dx\right\|_{L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}\left\|\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond k}\,dx\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond k}(x)(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond k}(y)\right]\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)(x)(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)(y)\right]^{k}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}G_{N}^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)^{k}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}\big(1+|\log(\mathbf{d}(x,y)+N^{-1})|)^{k}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,

uniformly in
N
, where we used successively the Wiener chaos estimate (
2.3
), the algebraic rule (
2.1
) and the estimate (
4.6
). This proves the uniform boundedness in
L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
.

Now to prove the Cauchy property, take
N_{2}\geq N_{1}\geq 1
and following the lines of Proposition
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathcal{M}}\big((\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}u)^{\diamond k}-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u)^{\diamond k}\big)\,dx\right\|_{L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}\left\|\int_{\mathcal{M}}\big((\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}u)^{\diamond k}-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u)^{\diamond k}\big)\,dx\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big((\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}u)^{\diamond k}-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u)^{\diamond k}\big)(x)\big((\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}u)^{\diamond k}-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}u)^{\diamond k}\big)(y)\Big]\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}^{2}}\Big(G_{N_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)^{k}-G_{N_{1}N_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)^{k}-G_{N_{2}N_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)^{k}+G_{N_{2}}^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)^{k}\Big)\,dx\,dy.

From there, the estimate (
2.4
) from the proof of Proposition
2.4
ensures that the right-hand side vanishes as
N_{1}\to+\infty
, proving that
\left(\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx\right)_{N}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
.
∎

Write
R_{N}(u):=e^{-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx}
for the exponential density function. Note that as a direct consequence of Proposition
2.6
, we get the convergence of
\left(R_{N}(u)\right)_{N}
almost surely with respect to
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
as a continuous function of the almost sure convergent sequence
\left(-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx\right)_{N}
. To conclude that the convergence also happens in
L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
for
p\geq 1
, fix
N_{1},N_{2}\geq 1
and
p\geq 1
. Denote by
A_{N_{1},N_{2},\eta}
the event

A_{N_{1},N_{2},\eta}:=\left\{\left|R_{N_{1}}(u)-R_{N_{2}}(u)\right|>\eta\right\}

then convergence in measure of
R_{N}(u)
ensures that for any
\eta>0
,

\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(A_{N_{1},N_{2},\eta})\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad N_{1},N_{2}\to+\infty.

Thus

\displaystyle{}\left\|R_{N_{1}}(u)-R_{N_{2}}(u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(R_{N_{1}}(u)-R_{N_{2}}(u)\right)\mathbf{1}_{A_{N_{1},N_{2},\eta}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}

(2.7)

\displaystyle+\left\|\left(R_{N_{1}}(u)-R_{N_{2}}(u)\right)\mathbf{1}_{A_{N_{1},N_{2},\eta}^{c}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}

\displaystyle\leq\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(A_{N_{1},N_{2},\eta})^{\frac{1}{2p}}\left\|R_{N_{1}}(u)-R_{N_{2}}(u)\right\|_{L^{2p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}+\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(A^{c}_{N_{1},N_{2},\eta})^{\frac{1}{p}}\eta.

From there, we see that a uniform bound on
R_{N}(u)
in
L^{2p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
is enough to get convergence of
R_{N}(u)
in
L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
. This will be provided by the Boué-Dupuis’ formula in the form introduced in
[
58
]
and
[
5
]
. Roughly speaking, given a cylindrical Brownian motion
X=(X_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
on
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
and under some assumptions on the non-linear functional
J:C([0,1];C^{(-1)^{-}}(\mathcal{M}))\to\mathbb{R}
, then the following variational formula holds:

(2.8)

\displaystyle{}\log\,\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-J(X)}\right]=\sup_{v}\,\mathbb{E}\left[-J\left(X+\int_{0}^{\cdot}v(t)\,dt\right)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|v(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{M})}^{2}\,dt\right],

where the supremum is taken over all the progressively measurable processes
v\in L^{2}(\Omega;L^{2}([0,1];L^{2}(\mathcal{M})))
. This allows to bound uniformly the partition function
\mathcal{Z}_{N}
.

Proposition 2.7
.

The normalization constant
\mathcal{Z}_{N}=\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\left[R_{N}(u)\right]
is uniformly bounded :

\sup_{N}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\left[R_{N}(u)\right]<+\infty.

Proof.

The assumption on
J
is to be
tame
(see
[
5
, Definition 1]
) with respect to the law of
X
for which an easy sufficient condition is to be bounded below and have some finite moment. Consider therefore the functional

J_{N}(X):=\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathcal{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{t=1})\,dx.

First of all note that
J_{N}
is well defined and measurable on
C([0,1];\mathcal{C}^{-1-\kappa}(\mathcal{M}))
by composition, for any
\kappa>0
. Since by assumption
F
is a polynomial of even degree and positive leading coefficient, so is
F_{N}^{\diamond}
and therefore
J_{N}
is bounded from below for each fixed
N
.

Then, note that the law of
u=\mathcal{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{t=1}
is exactly the one of the GFF
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
in (
1.5
), for which we already proved that

\int_{\mathcal{M}}F_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)\,dx

is bounded uniformly in
L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
, see Proposition
2.6
. Therefore, the functional
J_{N}
is tame in the sense of
[
5
]
, that is

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[e^{-qJ}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[|J|^{p}\right]<+\infty

for some conjugate exponents
p,q>1
, and Boué-Dupuis’ formula holds true in our context. As the supremum runs over the progressively measurable processes
v\in L^{2}(\mathbb{P};L^{2}([0,1];L^{2}(\mathcal{M})))
, we define the process
\Theta
by

\Theta(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v(s)\,ds,

so that
\dot{\Theta}=\mathcal{H}^{-\frac{1}{2}}v
and identity (
2.8
) writes

(2.9)

\displaystyle{}\log\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\left[R_{N}(u)\right]=\sup_{\Theta}\,\mathbb{E}\left[-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F^{\diamond}_{N}\left(\mathbf{P}_{N}u+\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1}\right)\,dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|\dot{\Theta}\|_{D\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}\right)}^{2}\,dt\right].

Even though
F
has positive leading coefficient,
F_{N}^{\diamond}
is not bounded from below uniformly in
N
. We thus expand the non-linearity using the binomial rule in Lemma
2.2
to get

\displaystyle F^{\diamond}_{N}\left(\mathbf{P}_{N}u+\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{|t=1}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{2m}a_{p}H_{p}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u+\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{|t=1},\sigma_{N}^{2})

(2.10)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{2m}\sum_{j=0}^{p}a_{p}\binom{p}{j}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{|t=1})^{p-j}

\displaystyle=F(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{|t=1})+\sum_{p=1}^{2m}\sum_{j=1}^{p}a_{p}\binom{p}{j}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{|t=1})^{p-j}.

The assumption that
F
is bounded below is crucial here as the contribution from
j=0
together with the quadratic part in (
2.9
),

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})\,dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|\dot{\Theta}\|_{D\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}\right)}^{2}\,dt,

will control all the cross product terms in between. The duality pairing between
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
B^{\varepsilon}_{1,1}\supset W^{\varepsilon,1}
yields

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})^{p-j}\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})}\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})^{p-j}\|_{W^{\varepsilon,1}(\mathcal{M})}.

At this point, the only remaining difficulty is to deal with the terms involving
\Theta
in (
2.10
). For the sake of clarity in the following estimates, let
v
be a generic element of
W^{\varepsilon,1}(\mathcal{M})
. First use the interpolation inequality

\|v\|_{W^{\varepsilon,1}}\lesssim\|v\|_{W^{1-\eta,1}}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\eta}}\|v\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1-\eta-\varepsilon}{1-\eta}}

for
\eta\in(0,1)
small enough. Together with Young’s inequality, this yields

\|v\|_{W^{\varepsilon,1}}\lesssim\|v\|_{W^{1-\eta,1}}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\eta}r}+\|v\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1-\eta-\varepsilon}{1-\eta}r^{\prime}}

for some
\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}=1
to be chosen later. Recall the fractional Leibniz rule from Lemma
A.4
:

\|uv\|_{H^{t}}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}}\|v\|_{H^{r}}

as long as
t=r+s-1
satisfy
0<r+s
and
r,s<1
. Then, taking
r=s=1-\frac{\eta}{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\|v^{p-j}\|_{H^{1-\eta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v^{p-j-1}\|_{H^{1-\frac{\eta}{2}}}\|v\|_{H^{1-\frac{\eta}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{j}\|v\|_{H^{1-\frac{\eta}{2^{p-j}}}}^{p-j}

and combined with the embedding
H^{1-\eta}(\mathcal{M})\hookrightarrow W^{1-\eta,1}(\mathcal{M})
and
L^{2m}(\mathcal{M})\hookrightarrow L^{p-j}(\mathcal{M})
as
\mathcal{M}
is compact together with the fact that the form domain
D(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})
embeds in any
H^{1-\kappa}(\mathcal{M})
, we get

\displaystyle\|v^{p-j}\|_{W^{\varepsilon,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{j}\|v\|_{H^{1-\frac{\eta}{2^{p-j}}}}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r}+\|v^{p-j}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1-\eta-\varepsilon}{1-\eta}r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{j}\|v\|_{D(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r}+\|v\|_{L^{2m}}^{\frac{1-\eta-\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r^{\prime}}.

Note that
H^{1-}(\mathcal{M})
itself is not an algebra, hence the loss of regularity when performing the product estimate. However, Leibniz rule allows to still have a nice estimate in spaces below
H^{1}(\mathcal{M})
and in the end, an estimate involving only the norm on
D(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})
.

Recall that the goal is to deal with the supremum over
\Theta
, hence the terms involving
u
are harmless as they are bounded in
L^{p}(\Omega,\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
for any
p>1,\varepsilon>0
and thus provide bounds uniformly in
N
. For
\delta>0
and
s>1
, we use once again Young’s inequality to have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})^{p-j}\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})}\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})^{p-j}\|_{W^{\varepsilon,1}(\mathcal{M})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})}\|\Theta_{t=1}\|_{D(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1}\|_{L^{2m}}^{\frac{1-\eta-\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta)\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})}^{\frac{s}{s-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta^{s}\|\Theta_{t=1}\|_{D(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)rs}+\delta^{s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1}\|_{L^{2m}}^{\frac{1-\eta-\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r^{\prime}s}.

Note that we dropped the regularization
\mathbf{P}_{N}
in the
\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1}\|_{H^{1-\frac{\eta}{2^{p-j}}}}
term as it maps usual Sobolev spaces to themselves, uniformly in
N
. In view of (
2.9
), to prove uniform boundedness we only need to tune the parameters so that the
D(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})
norm (resp.
L^{2m}
) is at most to the power
2
(resp.
2m
), hence the following conditions

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r<2,

\displaystyle\frac{1-\eta-\varepsilon}{1-\eta}(p-j)r^{\prime}<2m.

As
p-j<2m
for
j\neq 0
, the second condition is satisfied provided
r
is large enough. For such
r
being fixed, we just need to take
\varepsilon
small enough so that the first condition is satisfied as well. This ensures that for some
s>1
close enough to 1, the Young’s estimate above, together with
\delta
chosen small enough, make the cross product terms be absorbed by

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})\,dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|\dot{\Theta}\|_{D\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}\right)}^{2}\,dt.

Indeed, gathering everything together we get

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F^{\diamond}_{N}\left(\mathbf{P}_{N}u+\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1}\right)\,dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|\dot{\Theta}\|_{D\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}\right)}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt=-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})\,dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|\dot{\Theta}\|_{D\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}\right)}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\sum_{p=1}^{2m}\sum_{j=1}^{p}a_{p}\binom{p}{j}\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})^{p-j}\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq-\int_{\mathcal{M}}F(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})\,dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\|\dot{\Theta}\|_{D\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}\right)}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\delta^{s}\|\Theta_{t=1}\|_{D(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})}^{2}+\delta^{s}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1}\|_{L^{2m}}^{2m}+C(\delta)\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})}^{\frac{s}{s-1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq-\int_{\mathcal{M}}\big(F(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})-\delta^{s}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\Theta_{t=1})^{2m}\big)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\big(\frac{1}{2}-\delta^{s}\big)\int_{0}^{1}\|\dot{\Theta}\|_{D\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}}\right)}^{2}\,dt+C(\delta)\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{\diamond j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})}^{\frac{s}{s-1}}.

Taking
\delta
small enough ensures that the first two terms are bounded above by a deterministic constant, uniformly in
N
and
\Theta
. Taking the expectation, the last term is harmless as
u
is distributed according to
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
for which we already proved uniform bounds for the renormalized polynomials, this concludes the proof.
∎

Note that from the definition of
R_{N}
, then
(R_{N})^{p}
only amounts for
p\geq 1
to scaling the non linear functional
F
by a factor
p
so that the above argument still holds for any order moment and we have

\sup_{N}\left\|R_{N}(u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}<+\infty.

Combined with estimate (
2.3
) we get the following proposition.

Proposition 2.8
.

For any
p\geq 1
,
(R_{N})_{N}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
.

We can now give the proof of our first result.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Since for all
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the measures
\rho_{N}
are all defined by their density
\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{-1}R_{N}
with respect to the same measure
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
, and those density converge in
L^{1}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})
, it ensures the convergence in total variation of
(\rho_{N})_{N}
to some measure
\rho
that is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
.
∎

For our almost sure globalization argument, we will also need a bound uniform with respect to both a truncation with respect to
\Delta
and one with respect to
\mathcal{H}
.

Proposition 2.9
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
be supported in
(-1;1)
, and for
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
set
\chi_{M}=\chi(M^{-2}\mathcal{H})
. Then

\sup_{N,M}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\left[R_{N}(\chi_{M}u)\right]<+\infty.

Proof.

The proof follows from repeating verbatim that of Proposition
2.7
up to replacing
\Theta
and
u
by
\chi_{M}\Theta
and
\chi_{M}u
, and using that
\chi_{M}
is uniformly bounded on
\mathcal{D}(\sqrt{\mathcal{H}})
, and the last part of Proposition
2.5
instead of Proposition
2.6
.
∎

3.
Well-posedness theory

Now that the measure
\rho
has been constructed, we study a parabolic dynamics leaving it invariant. Similarly to
\rho
, the dynamics will rather be defined at a truncated level
N
and then passed to the limit. We therefore investigate the truncated dynamics (
1.10
) that we recall here

\partial_{t}u_{N}+\mathcal{H}u_{N}+\mathbf{P}_{N}f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u_{N})=\sqrt{2}\zeta

with
u(0)=u_{0}
. Note that we decide to truncate the non-linearity rather than the noise and initial data. This is mainly because the spectral projector
\mathbf{P}_{N}
does not commute with
\mathcal{H}
; see Remark
1.6
.

In this section we prove convergence of the smoothened dynamics (
1.10
) to a dynamics leaving
\rho
invariant, as well as deterministic local well-posedness and probabilistic global well-posedness results.

3.1.
Construction of the dynamics

We study the dynamics

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u_{N}+\mathcal{H}u_{N}+\mathbf{P}_{N}f^{\diamond}_{N}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u_{N})=\sqrt{2}\zeta\\
u_{N}(0)=u_{0}\end{cases}

for some rough initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
. As we investigate the invariance of the measure,
u_{0}
is intended to be distributed with law
\rho
. Since
\rho
is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
which is supported in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
, it is enough to treat the case where
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
.

We follow the usual Da Prato-Debussche trick from
[
14
]
: split the equation into a linear dynamics driven by
\zeta
and a non-linear dynamics starting from zero initial data. To that end we write

3.2.
Deterministic local well-posedness

We investigate the well-posedness of (
1.10
) for deterministic initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}(\mathcal{M})
. In that case, we can postulate

u_{N}=\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

where
w_{N}
solves the non-linear equation

(3.4)

\displaystyle{}\begin{cases}\partial_{t}w_{N}+\mathcal{H}w_{N}+\mathbf{P}_{N}f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}w_{N}+\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

w_{N}(0)=u_{0}-\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(0).\end{cases}

In view of the Schauder estimate (
4.3
) for
\mathcal{H}
, and the initial condition belonging to
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
, one expects
w_{N}
to belong to the following space:

(3.5)

\displaystyle X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}=C([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))\cap C((0,T],\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})),

endowed with the norm

\|w\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}=\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}+\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\epsilon}{2}}\|w(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}},

for
\varepsilon<\sigma<1
that takes account of the blow-up at
t=0
. The unusual restriction
\sigma<1
instead of
\sigma<2
in the case of the standard Laplace operator is due to the roughness of the Anderson Hamiltonian. One can prove that the solution belongs to the
L^{2}
-based Sobolev space
\mathcal{D}^{2-}
associated to
\mathcal{H}
which, only embeds in
C^{1-}
. Note that even if (
3.4
) is a truncated equation, as
\mathbf{P}_{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+|b_{0}|\int_{0}^{T}\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\leq Cr+C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{2m-1}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}(2m-1)},

for some constants

independant of

,

, and

. Thus, taking

with
\theta=\big(\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}(2m-1)\big)^{-1}>0
, we get

Similarly as above,

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\big\|\Phi_{(w_{0},\hbox to2.61pt{\vbox to5.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.20416pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\sum_{p=0}^{2m-1}\Big\{|b_{0}|\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{p}|b_{p}|\|w\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}^{j}\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{(p-j)}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}q^{\prime}}s^{-\big(\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}\big)jq^{\prime}}ds\Big)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq Cr+C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{2m-1}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}(2m-1)},

where we used the elementary integral inequality

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-a}s^{-b}ds\lesssim t^{1-a-b}

for any
0<a,b<1
with
a+b>1
.
Putting the two estimates together with the choice of
T
yields

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi_{(w_{0},\hbox to2.61pt{\vbox to5.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.20416pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 5.81}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 5.81 M 1.39 5.81 C 1.39 6.5 0.83 7.06 0.14 7.06 C -0.55 7.06 -1.11 6.5 -1.11 5.81 C -1.11 5.12 -0.55 4.56 0.14 4.56 C 0.83 4.56 1.39 5.12 1.39 5.81 Z M 0.14 5.81}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})}(w)\big\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}\leq 4Cr.

As for the time continuity, take
0<t\leq T
and
h
small enough, then split the integral

\displaystyle\Phi_{(w_{0},\hbox to2.61pt{\vbox to5.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.20416pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

maps the ball of radius

\lxSVG@closescope }

\displaystyle\leq C\|w_{0}-\widetilde{w_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{2m-2}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}(2m-1)}\|w-\widetilde{w}\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C^{\prime}(1+r)^{2m-2}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}(2m-1)}\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}-\widetilde{\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}.

This shows the Lipschitz dependence on
(w_{0},\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})
.
∎

With the local well-posedness result of Proposition
3.1
, we can prove our main local well-posedness result of Theorem
1.2
.

where

is as in Proposition

. Then set

and

From the bounds (

) and (

) we get

\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega\setminus\Sigma_{R}\right)\leq Ce^{-cR^{\frac{1}{m}}}

for some constants

. Then Borel-Cantelli lemma gives that
\Sigma
is of full probability.

Given
R>0
, we get from Proposition
3.1
that there exists a time

and

, (

) admits a unique solution

\lxSVG@closescope }

Moreover, since
(\mathbf{P}_{N}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})^{\diamond k}\to\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

in

, the Lipschitz continuity property of the solution in Proposition
3.1
ensures that

converges in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
to the solution

to (
3.6
) with data
(u_{0}-\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(0),\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})
. Since
u_{N}=\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+w_{N}
, this shows that
u_{N}
converges in
\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
to
u=\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+w
.
∎

3.3.
Almost sure globalization and measure invariance

We now turn to the globalization problem of the truncated dynamics (
1.10
) for random initial data
u_{0}
with law given by
\rho_{N}
defined as in (
1.9
). Note that since Theorem
1.2
holds
\mathbb{P}
almost surely for any initial data in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
, in view of Proposition
2.4
, it then also holds
\rho\otimes\mathbb{P}
almost surely. Moreover, recall that Proposition
3.1
provides a blow-up alternative: since the local time of existence
T=T(\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}},\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\|_{L^{q}_{T_{0}}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})
only depends on
\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}
and
\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\|_{L^{q}_{T_{0}}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}
, for any
T_{0}>0
, if
T^{*}=T^{*}(u_{0},\omega)>0
is the maximal time of existence of
w_{N}
on
[0,T_{0}]
, then

(3.8)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t<T^{*}}\|w_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}=+\infty\qquad\textup{or}\qquad T^{*}=T_{0}.

As we intend to prove global well-posedness for random initial data, we first need to investigate measure invariance of
\rho_{N}
under the flow of the truncated equation (
1.10
). As
\mathbf{P}_{N}
does not make the truncated equation (
1.10
) a finite-dimensional SDE, we first study another truncated dynamics. Let
M>0
and set
\Pi_{M}
to be the orthogonal projection of the finite dimensional space
\textup{Span}\left\{\varphi_{n}\,,\ \lambda_{n}\leq M\right\}
, we denote by
d_{M}
its dimension, with
d_{M}\simeq M
according to Weyl’s law (
4.1
).
\Pi_{M}
being a sharp projection, we do not recover continuity in Hölder spaces, so we rather consider a smooth counterpart
\chi_{M}=\chi(M^{-2}\mathcal{H})
for some
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
compactly supported in
(-1;1)
. Note that
\mathbf{P}_{N}
is based on
\Delta
while
\chi_{M}
is defined with
\mathcal{H}
, hence both operators do not commute with one another. Still, we consider
u_{N,M}
to solve the following equation starting from some initial data
u_{0}
:

(3.9)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u_{N,M}+\mathcal{H}u_{N,M}+\chi_{M}\mathbf{P}_{N}f_{N}^{\diamond}\left(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}u_{N,M}\right)=\sqrt{2}\zeta,\\
u_{N,M}(0)=u_{0}.\end{cases}

Expanding
f_{N}^{\diamond}
to emphasis the dependency in
N
, we can rewrite (
3.9
) as

(3.10)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u_{N,M}+\mathcal{H}u_{N,M}+\sum_{p=0}^{2m-1}b_{p}\chi_{M}\mathbf{P}_{N}H_{p}\left(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}u_{N,M},\sigma_{N}\right)=\sqrt{2}\zeta,\\
u_{N,M}(0)=u_{0}.\end{cases}

Note that
\chi_{M}
acts on both the non-linearity and the
u_{N,M}
term inside, and since
\chi_{M}=\Pi_{M}\chi_{M}=\chi_{M}\Pi_{M}
by the support property of
\chi
, we see that
\Pi_{M}u_{N,M}
will solve a finite dimensional system of coupled SDEs while
(1-\Pi_{M})u_{N,M}
satisfies a linear evolution equation driven by a white noise. Namely, if we write
\Pi_{M}u_{N,M}(t)=\sum_{n=0}^{d_{M}}a_{N,M}^{(n)}(t)\varphi_{n}
, then for
0\leq n\leq d_{M}
we have

da_{N,M}^{(n)}+\left[\lambda_{n}a_{N,M}^{(n)}+\sum_{p=0}^{2m-1}b_{p}\left\langle\chi_{M}\mathbf{P}_{N}H_{p}\left(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\sum_{n=0}^{d_{M}}a_{N,M}^{(n)}\varphi_{n},\sigma_{N}\right),\varphi_{n}\right\rangle\right]dt=\sqrt{2}\,dB_{n}

for independent Brownian motions
B_{n}
. This can be seen as a finite dimensional stochastic gradient flow

da^{(n)}+\partial_{a^{(n)}}\mathcal{E}_{N,M}(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})})\,dt=\sqrt{2}\,dB_{n}\quad\text{for}\ 0\leq n\leq d_{M}

with truncated energy

\mathcal{E}_{N,M}(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})}):=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{d_{M}}\lambda_{n}(a^{(n)})^{2}+\sum_{p=0}^{2m-1}\frac{b_{p}}{p+1}\int_{\mathcal{M}}H_{p+1}\left(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\sum_{n=0}^{d_{M}}a_{N,M}^{(n)}\varphi_{n},\sigma_{N}\right)\,dx.

To this finite dimensional system correspond the generator
\mathcal{L}_{N,M}
and Gibbs measure
\rho_{N,M}
defined respectively by

\mathcal{L}_{N,M}:=\sum_{n=0}^{d_{M}}\partial_{a^{(n)}}^{2}-\partial_{a^{(n)}}\mathcal{E}_{N,M}(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})})\partial_{a^{(n)}}

and

\displaystyle d\rho_{N,M}(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})})

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{Z}_{N,M}^{-1}e^{-\mathcal{E}_{N,M}(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})})}\,da^{(0)}\cdots da^{(d_{M})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{N,M}^{-1}R_{N,M}(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})})d((\Pi_{M})_{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}})(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})}),

with normalization constant
\mathcal{Z}_{N,M}
. Integrating
\mathcal{L}_{N,M}f
against
d\rho_{N,M}
for some test function
f
we get (denoting
a^{(0-d_{M})}=(a^{(0)},\cdots,a^{(d_{M})})
)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}\mathcal{L}_{N,M}f(a^{(0-d_{M})})\,d\rho_{N,M}(a^{(0-d_{M})})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{N,M}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}e^{-\mathcal{E}_{N,M}(a^{(0-d_{M})})}\sum_{n=0}^{d_{M}}\left(\partial_{a^{(n)}}^{2}f(a^{(0-d_{M})})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad-\left.\partial_{a^{(n)}}\mathcal{E}_{N,M}\partial_{a^{(n)}}f(a^{(0-d_{M})})\right)\,da^{(0)}\cdots da^{(d_{M})}

\displaystyle=0

after integration by parts. This proves that
\Pi_{M}u_{N,M}
leaves the measure
\rho_{N,M}
invariant.

Now for remaining part
u_{N,M}^{\bot}:=(1-\Pi_{M})u_{N,M}
, the non-linear term disappears since
(1-\Pi_{M})\chi_{M}=0
by the support property of
\chi
, so that
u_{N,M}^{\bot}
satisfies the linear equation

\partial_{t}u_{N,M}^{\bot}+\mathcal{H}u_{N,M}^{\bot}=\sqrt{2}(1-\Pi_{M})\zeta.

This equation has as unique invariant measure
(1-\Pi_{M})_{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
. Therefore,
u_{N,M}=\Pi_{M}u_{N,M}+u_{N,M}^{\bot}
leaves the measure
\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})_{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
invariant. The only part missing to make this whole discussion rigorous is the global existence of a solution
u_{N,M}
in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
, hence the following proposition.

Proposition 3.2
.

For fixed
N,M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, and initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
, then the flow of (
3.10
) extends globally. Moreover the truncated measure
\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
is invariant under the flow of (
3.10
).

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\|_{L^{2m}}^{2m}+C_{j,p}(\delta)\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})^{\diamond j}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2m}{2m-p+j-1}}.

And then after a proper rescale of
\delta
:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{p=0}^{2m-1}\sum_{j=0}^{p}\mathbf{1}_{(j,p)\neq(0,2m-1)}\binom{p}{j}b_{p}\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}w)^{p-j+1}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})^{\diamond j}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\delta\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}w\|_{L^{2m}}^{2m}+C(\delta,T,N,M),

for some constant
C
. Then, as our non-linearity is defocusing (
b_{2m-1}>0
), integration in time yields

\displaystyle\Psi_{N,M}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\delta\Psi_{N,M}(t)+C(\delta,T,N,M),

and then
\Psi_{N,M}(t)
is bounded on
[0,T]
. Since
\Psi_{N,M}
controls the
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
norm of
w=\Pi_{M}w_{N,M}
, we obtain global existence of
w_{N,M}
, and then global existence of
u_{N,M}
.

Now that we have proved that (
3.10
) is globally well-posed, the previous discussion ensures that
\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
is invariant under the flow of (
3.10
).
∎

We now turn to the limiting procedure by taking
M,N\to+\infty
. At first we let
M\to+\infty
, this will prove the following bound on the local flow
\Phi_{N}
of (
1.10
).

Lemma 3.3
.

For any
T>0
and
N\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C_{T}
uniform in
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that

(3.12)

\displaystyle{}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T\wedge T^{*}}\big\|\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]\leq C_{T},

where
T^{*}=T^{*}_{N}(u_{0},\omega)
is the maximal time of existence of
u_{N}=\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)
.

T>0
and

, we first prove a similar bound for
\Phi_{N,M}
the flow of (

). From Proposition

,
