\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\omega)(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\|f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0},\omega)(s))\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\,ds,

where we used successively Corollary
4.8
and Lemma
A.5
for the boundedness properties of
\chi_{M}
and
\mathbf{P}_{N}
on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})
. Note that
u_{0}\sim\rho_{N}
, so in particular we also have
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
almost surely, and thus also
\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0})(t)\in\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
, so that the last term above makes sense. Integrating with respect to
\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}
, Fubini’s theorem and invariance of
\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
under
\Phi_{N,M}
allow to deal with the integral in the right hand side:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\|f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0},\omega)(s))\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\,ds\,\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\Big[\|f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0},\omega)(s))\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\Big]ds

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=T\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\Big[\|f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=T\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\Big[\mathcal{Z}^{-1}_{N,M}R_{N}(\chi_{M}u_{0})\|f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq T\Big\|\|f_{N}^{\diamond}(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}\Big\|\mathcal{Z}^{-1}_{N,M}R_{N}(\chi_{M}u_{0})\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu^{\mathcal{H}})}.

From there, we invoke Propositions
2.5
and
2.9
to get a bound uniform in both
N
and
M
.

For the other part, note that
e^{-t\mathcal{H}}\big(u_{0}-\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\omega)(0)\big)+\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\omega)(t)
is explicitly given by

e^{-t\mathcal{H}}\big(u_{0}-\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\omega)(0)\big)+\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(\omega)(t)=e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\,\zeta(ds).

We treat the propagator in a similar way we did for the Duhamel integral

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\Big[\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\Big[\|u_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\Big[\mathcal{Z}_{N,M}^{-2}R_{N}(\chi_{M}u_{0})^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}},

and again the right hand side is uniformly bounded in
N,M
. For the remaining part, we use the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality and finally get

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\,\zeta(ds)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]\leq C_{T}

for some constant
C_{T}
uniform in
N
and
M
. This proves that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]\leq C_{T}.

Now, from
C_{T}
being uniform in
N,M
, the convergence almost surely and in
L^{p}(\Omega)
of
(\mathbf{P}_{N}\chi_{M}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})^{\diamond k}
to
(\mathbf{P}_{N}\hbox to2.78pt{\vbox to7.49pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.29164pt\lower-0.09999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})^{\diamond k}
in
L^{q}([0;T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
(Proposition
2.5
) together with the continuity property of the flow given by Proposition
3.1
, and the convergence in total variation of
\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
to
\rho_{N}
due to Propositions
2.5
and
2.9
, we can use Fatou’s lemma and pass
M
to the limit so that all in all we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T\wedge T^{*}}\|\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\liminf_{M\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T\wedge T^{*}}\|\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{T},

again uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
∎

In view of the blow-up criterion (
3.8
) for the equation (
3.1
), thus for the equation (
1.10
), the estimate (
3.12
) is enough to prove probabilistic global well-posedness of the truncated renormalized dynamics (
1.10
).

Proposition 3.4
.

For any
N\geq 1
, (
1.10
) is
\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely globally well posed. Moreover the truncated Gibbs measure
\rho_{N}
is invariant under the flow
\Phi_{N}
of (
1.10
), that is, for any
t\geq 0
and
\mathfrak{F}\in C_{b}(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
,

\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\right]\Big]=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[u_{0}\right]\Big].

Proof.

Fix
N\geq 1
, then (
3.12
) reads as, uniformly in
N
, for each
T
there exists an event
\Sigma_{N,T}
of full
\rho_{N}^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-measure such that

\|\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\leq C_{T}

for any
(u_{0},\omega)\in\Sigma_{N,T}
. Specializing to times
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, then the blow-up alternative ensures that
\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)
is well defined at least up to time
k
. Taking the countable intersection, we see that

\Sigma_{N}:=\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\Sigma_{N,T_{k}}

is of full
\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-measure and that
\Phi_{N}
is defined globally for any
(u_{0},\omega)\in\Sigma_{N}
.

Now for the invariance part, pick
t\geq 0
and
\mathfrak{F}\in C_{b}(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
.
As in Proposition
2.7
, we write
\rho_{N}
as

d\rho_{N}(u)=\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{-1}R_{N}(u)\,d\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(u).

Then from the convergence in total variation of
\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
to
\rho_{N}
and that of
\Phi_{N,M}
to
\Phi_{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\right]\Big]=\lim_{M\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\right]\Big],

and from Proposition
3.2
we can deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\right]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[\Phi_{N,M}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\right]\Big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{M\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N,M}\otimes(1-\Pi_{M})^{*}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[u_{0}\right]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[u_{0}\right]\Big],

proving that
\rho_{N}
is invariant under the flow
\Phi_{N}
.
∎

Combining now the a priori estimate of Proposition (
3.12
) together with the local convergence of
\Phi_{N}
to
\Phi
(Theorem
1.2
) and that of
\rho_{N}
to
\rho
(Proposition
2.4
), we can finally get our almost sure global well-posedness result.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Recall that the constant in (
3.12
) is uniform in
N
. Since Propositions
3.1
and
1.1
ensure convergence of
\Phi_{N}
and
\rho_{N}
respectively almost surely and in total variation, Fatou’s lemma yet again yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T\wedge T^{*}}\|\Phi(u_{0},\omega)(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{N\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T\wedge T^{*}}\|\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{T},

where
T^{*}=T^{*}(u_{0},\omega)
is the maximal time of existence of
u=\Phi(u_{0},\omega)
. As before, this yields the existence of a set
5
5

5

Recall that
\Sigma\subset\Omega
is the set defined in Theorem
1.2
where almost sure local well-posedness of (
1.11
) holds.

\tilde{\Sigma}\subset\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})\times\Sigma
of full
\rho\otimes\mathbb{P}
-measure such that
\Phi(u_{0},\omega)
is defined globally for any
(u_{0},\omega)\in\tilde{\Sigma}
. The invariance of
\rho
then follows again from dominated convergence, the convergence of
\Phi_{N}
to
\Phi
(Theorem
1.2
), the convergence of
\rho_{N}
to
\rho
(Theorem
1.1
), and the invariance of
\rho_{N}
under the flow
\Phi_{N}
(Proposition
3.4
): for any
t\geq 0
and
\mathfrak{F}\in C_{b}(\mathcal{C}^{-\epsilon}(\mathcal{M}))
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[\Phi(u_{0},\omega)(t)\right]\Big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[\Phi_{N}(u_{0},\omega)(t)\right]\Big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{N}}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[u_{0}\right]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\Big[\mathfrak{F}\left[u_{0}\right]\Big]

This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.3
.
∎

4.
A singular case: the Anderson Hamiltonian

In this section, we explain the construction of the Anderson Hamiltonian and state its properties used throughout this work. It was first constructed by Allez and Chouk
[
1
]
on the two-dimensional torus and then extended to various framework, see
[
25
,
31
,
34
]
and references therein. It is the Schrödinger operator

\mathcal{H}=-\Delta+\xi

with potential the spatial white noise on
\mathcal{M}
. As explained, the white noise is a random distribution of negative Hölder regularity and belongs almost surely to
\mathcal{C}^{-1-\kappa}
for any
\kappa>0
. In one dimension, the white noise is the derivative of the Brownian motion and was first constructed by Paley and Zygmund
[
46
,
47
]
. In this case,
\xi\in\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\kappa}
and the quadratic form
\langle(-\partial_{x}^{2}+\xi)u,u\rangle
is well-defined with domain
H^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, see Fukushima and Nakao
[
22
]
. Having a precise description of its domain and its large volume limit was the subject of recent work by Dumaz and Labbé, see
[
21
]
and references therein. In two dimensions, this operator is singular in the sense that a renormalisation procedure is needed to make sense of
\mathcal{H}
. This is hinted by the fact that the product
u\xi
is singular for
u\in H^{1}(\mathcal{M})
hence the quadratic form is not well-defined. Following the recent progress in singular SPDEs, the operator can be defined as the limit of the renormalised operator

\mathcal{H}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}(-\Delta+\xi_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})

with
\xi_{\varepsilon}
a mollification of the noise and
c_{\varepsilon}
a logarithmic diverging quantity. Following the idea of paracontrolled calculus from Gubinelli, Imkeller and Perkowski
[
24
]
, one can consider function
u
paracontrolled by a functionnal of the noise as domain for the operator or its quadratic form.

4.1.
Construction of the operator

Consider the space

\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}:=\{u\in L^{2}(\mathcal{M})\ ;\ u-\mathsf{P}_{u}X\in H^{\sigma}\}

with
X_{1}
solution to
\Delta X_{1}=\xi
and
\mathsf{P}
a paraproduct for
\sigma\geq 1
. The notion of paraproduct goes back to Bony
[
7
]
based on the Littlewood-Paley decomposition and can be extended to manifold, following for example
[
34
]
. The main idea is one can decompose a product as

uv=\mathsf{P}_{u}v+\mathsf{\Pi}(u,v)+\mathsf{P}_{v}u

for any distribution
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
and
v\in\mathcal{C}^{\beta}
where the resonant term
\mathsf{\Pi}(u,v)
is well-defined only if
\alpha+\beta>0
, this is Young condition. Moreoever, this decomposition allows for a precise track of regularity with the continuity result

\|\mathsf{P}_{u}v\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge 0+\beta}_{p,q}}\lesssim\|u\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge 0}_{p_{1},\infty}}\|v\|_{B^{\beta}_{p_{2},q}}

for any
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
1\leq p_{1},p_{2},p,q\leq\infty
such that
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}
, and

\|\mathsf{\Pi}(u,b)\|_{B^{\alpha+\beta}_{p,q}}\lesssim\|u\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p_{1},\infty}}\|v\|_{B^{\beta}_{p_{2},q}}

for any
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\alpha+\beta>0
. The idea motivating the introduction of
\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}
lies in the algebraic cancellation

\displaystyle-\Delta u+u\xi

\displaystyle=-\Delta\mathsf{P}_{u}X_{1}-\Delta u^{\sharp}+\mathsf{P}_{u}\xi+\mathsf{\Pi}(u,\xi)+\mathsf{P}_{\xi}u

\displaystyle=\mathsf{P}_{u}(-\Delta X+\xi)-\Delta u^{\sharp}+[\Delta,\mathsf{P}_{u}]X_{1}+\mathsf{\Pi}(u,\xi)+\mathsf{P}_{\xi}u

\displaystyle=-\Delta u^{\sharp}+[\Delta,\mathsf{P}_{u}]X_{1}+\mathsf{\Pi}(u,\xi)+\mathsf{P}_{\xi}u

where
u=\mathsf{P}_{u}X_{1}+u^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}
. This shows that introducing roughness in
u
via the paracontrolled expansion cancels exactly the most irregular term in
\mathcal{H}u
. However, this introduces a new singular product of distribution in
\mathsf{\Pi}(u,\xi)
. Indeed,
\xi\in\mathcal{C}^{-1-\kappa}
thus
X_{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}
hence the sum of the regularity exponent
-1-\kappa+1-\kappa=-2\kappa<0
barely fails Young condition. This is the singular nature of the operator and
[
24
]
introduced the corrector
\mathsf{C}
to write

\mathsf{\Pi}(u,\xi)=\mathsf{\Pi}(\mathsf{P}_{u}X_{1},\xi)+\mathsf{\Pi}(u^{\sharp},\xi)=u\mathsf{\Pi}(X_{1},\xi)+\mathsf{C}(u,X_{1},\xi)+\mathsf{\Pi}(u^{\sharp},\xi)

for
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}
. For
\sigma>1
, the resonant product
\mathsf{\Pi}(u^{\sharp},\xi)
is well-defined and one can prove that
\mathsf{C}
is a continuous trilinear operator from
H^{\alpha}\times\mathcal{C}^{\beta}\times\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}
to
H^{\alpha+\beta+\gamma}
for
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\beta+\gamma<0<\alpha+\beta+\gamma<1.

The condition
\beta+\gamma<0
corresponds exactly to the singular nature of the product
X_{1}\xi
hence
\mathsf{C}(u,X_{1},\xi)
is well-defined as a function as soon as
u
is regular enough. This does not change that the resonant product
\mathsf{\Pi}(X_{1},\xi)
is singular hence the need for a renormalisation procedure, this can be done with the Wick product

\mathsf{\Pi}(X_{1},\xi)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Big(\mathsf{\Pi}(X_{1,\varepsilon},\xi_{\varepsilon})-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathsf{\Pi}(X_{1,\varepsilon},\xi_{\varepsilon})\big]\Big)

with
\xi_{\varepsilon}
a mollification of the noise and
\Delta X_{1,\varepsilon}=\xi_{\varepsilon}
. After this procedure, one obtains that
\mathcal{H}
is well-defined on
\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}
for
\sigma>1
and

\|\mathcal{H}u+\Delta u^{\sharp}\|_{H^{-\kappa}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{1+\varepsilon}}

for any
\kappa,\varepsilon>0
. In particular, the form quadratic
\langle\mathcal{H}u,u\rangle
is well-defined and one can prove

\langle\mathcal{H}u,u\rangle+c\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{1}}^{2}\geq 0

for a random constant
c>0
using almost duality, see for example
[
34
, Proposition 2.9]
. At this point, one can prove that the quadratic form
\langle\mathcal{H}u,u\rangle
with domain
\mathcal{D}^{1}
is closed, symmetric, continuous and bounded from below thus its associated operator is self-adjoint. One can then obtain a second order paracontrolled expansion of the domain as

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})=\{u\in L^{2}(\mathcal{M})\ ;\ u-\mathsf{P}_{u}X\in H^{2}\}

with
X=X_{1}+X_{2}
and
X_{2}
solution to
\Delta X_{2}=\mathsf{\Pi}(X_{1},\xi)+\mathsf{P}_{\xi}X_{1}
, see
[
34
]
for this construction. The operator
\mathcal{H}
has a compact resolvent with discrete spectrum
\lambda_{0}<\lambda_{1}\leq\ldots
with a basis of eigenfunctions
(\varphi_{n})_{n\geq 1}
, see
[
45
]
for a simple argument of the spectral gap. In the following, we suppose that the operator is positive considering for example that we include in the renormalisation the shift by the random constant
-\lambda_{0}+1
. Moreover, it was proved in
[
34
]
that the sequence of eigenvalues satisfies the same Weyl law (
A.1
) as that of the Laplace-Beltrami operator, namely

(4.1)

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}\sim n.

The form domain
\mathcal{D}(\sqrt{H})=\mathcal{D}^{1}
is the reciproque image of
H^{1}
by the application

\Phi(u)=u-\mathsf{P}_{u}X

which is continuous from
H^{\sigma}
to itself for any
\sigma<1
. Up to a random truncation of the noise which does not change the regularity, one can prove that
\Phi:H^{\sigma}\to H^{\sigma}
is invertible as a perturbation of the identity, we denote by
\Gamma
its inverse. It can be seen at the implicit solution to

(4.2)

\Gamma u^{\sharp}=\mathsf{P}_{\Gamma u^{\sharp}}X+u^{\sharp}

for
u^{\sharp}\in L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
. The important idea is that the operator
\mathcal{H}\Gamma
is a better behaved perturbation of the identity at the price that it is not self-adjoint anymore. Namely

\mathcal{H}\Gamma u^{\#}=-\Delta u^{\#}+G_{\xi}(u^{\#})

where
G_{\xi}
is a bounded operator from
6
6

6

The cases
p=2
and
p=+\infty
are proved in
[
36
]
or
[
24
]
. The case
p=1
follows from a straightforward modification of the computation in
[
36
]
, and the case of general
p
then follows from interpolation.

W^{\sigma,p}
to
W^{\sigma-1-\kappa,p}
for any
\kappa>0
provided the resonant product
\mathsf{\Pi}(u^{\#},\xi)
is well defined, i.e.
\sigma>1+\kappa
.

We introduce the conjugaison of
\mathcal{H}
via the map
\Gamma
, that is

\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}=\Gamma^{-1}\mathcal{H}\Gamma.

In particular, we have

\|(\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}+\Delta)u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1+\varepsilon}}

for any
\kappa,\varepsilon>0
. This allows to compare the heat semigroup of associated to
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}
and
\Delta
with

e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}-e^{t\Delta}=\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\Delta}(\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}+\Delta)e^{-s\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}ds,

this is the main argument for the results in
[
2
]
. The same argument for the Schrödinger with a second order expansion yields Strichartz inequalities, see
[
36
]
. In particular, this perturbative argument gives Schauder estimates for the heat semigroup of
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}
, that is

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}

for
\alpha\in(1,2)
and
0<\alpha-\beta<2
, see
[
2
, Theorem 22]
. This can be used to obtain Schauder estimates for the heat semigroup associated to
\mathcal{H}
. This will be important to prove local well-posedness for the Anderson stochastic quantization equation for deterministic initial data.

Proposition 4.1
.

For any
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,1)
such that
\alpha>\beta
, we have

(4.3)

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}.

Proof.

First, one interpolates the bounds
\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}
and

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}

for
\alpha\in(1,2)
and
0<\alpha-\beta<2
. Let
\theta\in(0,1)
and consider

\gamma_{\theta}:=\theta\alpha+(1-\theta)\beta\in(\beta,\alpha).

Interpolation gives

\displaystyle\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}\to\mathcal{C}^{\gamma_{\theta}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}\to\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}^{1-\theta}\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}\to\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{\gamma_{\theta}-\beta}{2}}

since
\gamma_{\theta}-\beta=\theta\alpha+(1-\theta)\beta-\beta=(\alpha-\beta)\theta
hence

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}

for any
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,2)
such that
\alpha>\beta
. For the result for
\mathcal{H}
, we have

\displaystyle\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\|\Gamma e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}\Gamma^{-1}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}}\Gamma^{-1}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|\Gamma^{-1}u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}

using that
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{-1}
are continuous from
\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}
to itself for
\gamma<1
and the result for
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}
.
∎

Natural spaces are the general Sobolev spaces associated to
\mathcal{H}
defined as the closure of the vector space spanned by the eigenfunctions of
\mathcal{H}
with respect to the norm

(4.4)

{}\|u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}}:=\Big(\sum_{n\geq 1}\langle\lambda_{n}\rangle^{\sigma}|\langle u,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

for
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular, one immediatly has the Schauder estimates

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\alpha}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\beta}}

for
\alpha>\beta
. One can prove that
\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}=H^{\sigma}
for
|\sigma|<1
, thus

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u\|_{H^{\alpha}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{H^{\beta}}

for
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,1)
such that
\alpha>\beta
. With the previous result, this gives the general Schauder estimates

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u\|_{B_{p,p}^{\beta}}

for
p\in[2,\infty]
and
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,1)
such that
\alpha>\beta
however we should only use the case
p=2
or
p=\infty
in this work.

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
\sigma\in(-1,1)
and
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})
, then the map
t\mapsto e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u\in\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}
is continuous.

Proof.

First note that, by the semigroup property and (
4.3
), continuity at 0 is enough. Let
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})
and fix
\delta>0
. Then by density of
H^{2}(\mathcal{M})
in
\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})
and continuity of
\Gamma
, we have that
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
is dense in
\Gamma\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}(\mathcal{M})
. Thus there exists
v\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
such that
\|u-v\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}<\delta.
Then we have for
h\geq 0
small enough:

\displaystyle\big\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)u\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)(u-v)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}+\big\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)v\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}.

As for the first term, using the Schauder estimate (
4.3
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)(u-v)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\|u-v\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\delta,

uniformly in
h
.
For the second term, we use

\displaystyle\big\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)v\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)v\big\|_{\mathcal{D}^{1+\sigma+\kappa}}\lesssim h^{\frac{1-\sigma-\kappa}{2}}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}^{2}}.

Taking
h
small enough depending on
\delta
, this finally shows that

\displaystyle\big\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)u\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\leq C\delta.

∎

4.2.
Anderson Green’s function and GFF

In order to get a precise bound on the truncated Green function of the Anderson operator, we first show that
G^{\mathcal{H}}
and
G^{1-\Delta}
have the same singularity. This is done by comparing both resolvents.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
4\leq p<\infty
and
0<\delta<\frac{1}{2}
. Then
\mathcal{H}^{-1}-(1-\Delta)^{-1}
is bounded from
W^{-\frac{2}{p}-\delta,p^{\prime}}
to
W^{\frac{2}{p}+\delta,p}
. In particular
(G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta})(x,y)\in L^{\infty}_{y}W^{\frac{2}{p}+\delta,p}_{x}
, and
G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)\in L^{\infty}_{x}W^{\frac{2}{p}-\delta,p}_{y}
.

Proof.

\mathcal{H}
being self-adjoint and positive thus invertible, an application of the resolvent formula yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{-1}-(1-\Delta)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}-(1-\Delta)^{-1}+\mathcal{H}^{-1}-(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}((1-\Delta)-\mathcal{H}\Gamma)(1-\Delta)^{-1}+B(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}

following yet again the same paradigm as before:
\mathcal{H}
is a perturbation of
-\Delta
through the map
\Gamma
. By definition,
\Gamma
is a perturbation of the identity, namely

(4.5)

\Gamma=1+\sum_{k\geq 1}(\mathsf{P}_{\cdot}X)^{k}=:1+B.

Note that, as
X\in\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}
for any
\kappa>0
, we can write a nice continuity estimate for
B
. Indeed, for any
\nu\geq 0
and
p>1
,

\|\mathsf{P}_{u}X\|_{W^{1-\nu-\kappa,p}}\leq c(\nu,\kappa,p)\|u\|_{W^{-\nu,p}}\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}}

where
\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}}
can be made arbitrarily small up to a random truncation of
X
as in the definition of
\Gamma
. This proves that

B:W^{-\nu,p}\to W^{1-\nu-\kappa,p}

is a bounded operator for any
\kappa>0
and
\nu\geq 0
.

As for
(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}
, if
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\nu-2}
and
v=(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}u
is the solution to
\mathcal{H}\Gamma v=-\Delta v+G_{\xi}v=u
, we have for any
\nu\in(1;2]
by definition of
\mathcal{D}^{\nu}(\mathcal{H})=\Gamma^{-1}H^{\nu}
:

\displaystyle\|v\|_{H^{\nu}}

\displaystyle=\big\|\Gamma\mathcal{H}^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu}}=\big\|\mathcal{H}^{-1}u\big\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu}}=\|u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu-2}}\sim\|u\|_{H^{\nu-2}}

where in the last step we used that
\Gamma
is invertible on
H^{\sigma}
for
\sigma\in(-1;1)
.

Combining all this with the resolvent formula above we obtain some
\kappa>0
small enough:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{H}^{-1}u-(1-\Delta)^{-1}u\right\|_{W^{\frac{2}{p}+\delta,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}(1-G_{\xi})(1-\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{1+\delta}}+\big\|B(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}u\big\|_{W^{\frac{2}{p}+\delta,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|(1-G_{\xi})(1-\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{-1+\delta}}+\big\|(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}u\big\|_{W^{\frac{2}{p}+\delta+\kappa-1,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|(1-G_{\xi})(1-\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{W^{-\frac{2}{p}+\delta,p^{\prime}}}+\big\|(\mathcal{H}\Gamma)^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\delta+\kappa}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|(1-\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{W^{\max(-\frac{2}{p}+\delta,0)+1+2\kappa,p^{\prime}}}+\|u\|_{H^{\delta+\kappa-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{\max(-\frac{2}{p}+\delta,0)-1+2\kappa,p^{\prime}}}+\|u\|_{W^{\delta+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-1,p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{-\frac{2}{p}-\delta,p^{\prime}}},

where in the last step we used the restrictions on
p
and
\delta
in the statement of Lemma
4.3
. This shows the boundedness properties of
\mathcal{H}^{-1}-(1-\Delta)^{-1}
, thus the regularity of
G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta}
. The regularity of
G^{\mathcal{H}}
then follows from that of
G^{1-\Delta}
since
G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta}
is smoother.
∎

Next, we investigate the properties of the Green’s function and its smoothened counterparts. Let us recall the following fundamental lemma that can be found in
[
40
, Lemma 2.8 , 2.12 and 2.13]
:

Lemma 4.4
.

(i)
There exists
C>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
(x,y)\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathrm{diag}
it holds

(4.6)

\displaystyle\Big|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})G^{1-\Delta}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(\mathbf{d}(x,y)+N^{-1}\big)\Big|\leq C.

(ii)
For all
0<\delta\ll 1
there exists
C>0
such that for any
N_{1}\leq N_{2}
and
(x,y)\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathrm{diag}
it holds for any
j\in\{1;2\}
:

\displaystyle\Big|(\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}})G^{1-\Delta}(x,y)-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})G^{1-\Delta}(x,y)\Big|

(4.7)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C\min\Big\{-\log\big(\mathbf{d}(x,y)+N_{1}^{-1}\big)\vee 1;N_{1}^{\delta-1}\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{-1}\Big\}.

Combining Lemmas
4.3
and
4.4
, we get the following result.

Corollary 4.5
.

(i)
There exists
C>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
(x,y)\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathrm{diag}
it holds

(4.8)

\displaystyle\Big|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(\mathbf{d}(x,y)+N^{-1}\big)\Big|\leq C.

(ii)
For all
0<\delta\ll 1
there exists
C>0
such that for any
N_{1}\leq N_{2}
and
(x,y)\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathrm{diag}
it holds for any
j\in\{1;2\}
:

\displaystyle\Big|(\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{j}})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)\Big|

(4.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C\min\Big\{-\log\big(\mathbf{d}(x,y)+N_{1}^{-1}\big)\vee 1;N_{1}^{\delta-1}\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{-1}\Big\}.

Proof.

We start with the proof of (
4.8
). Due to (
4.6
), we have

\displaystyle(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)=(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})\big(G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta})(x,y)-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(\mathbf{d}(x,y)+N^{-1}\big)+O_{L^{\infty}}(1).

Since
\mathbf{P}_{N}
is uniformly bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
, we also have

\displaystyle\big\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})\big(G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta})\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1

uniformly in
N\geq 1
, due to Lemma
4.3
. This proves (
4.8
).

As for (
4.5
), we first show that

(4.10)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}-\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\big\|_{W^{\sigma+\delta,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}

for any
N_{1}\leq N_{2}
,
\delta\in[0;2]
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
, and
1\leq p\leq\infty
. Indeed, for any
\lambda_{n}
, we have from the mean value theorem

(4.11)

\displaystyle\psi(N_{1}^{-2}\lambda_{n})-\psi(N_{2}^{-2}\lambda_{n})=(N_{1}^{-2}-N_{2}^{-2})\lambda_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\psi^{\prime}\big((\theta N_{1}^{-2}+(1-\theta)N_{2}^{-2})\lambda_{n}\big)d\theta,

Since
\psi^{\prime}
is Schwartz, we get from (
4.11
) and (
A.7
) that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}-\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\big\|_{W^{\sigma+2,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}

\displaystyle\leq(N_{1}^{-2}-N_{2}^{-2})\int_{0}^{1}\big\|\Delta\psi^{\prime}\big((\theta N_{1}^{-2}+(1-\theta)N_{2}^{-2})\Delta\big)\big\|_{W^{\sigma+2,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}d\theta

(4.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-2}\int_{0}^{1}\big\|\psi^{\prime}\big((\theta N_{1}^{-2}+(1-\theta)N_{2}^{-2})\Delta\big)\big\|_{W^{\sigma+2,p}\to W^{\sigma+2,p}}d\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-2},

while using directly (
A.7
),

(4.13)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}-\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\big\|_{W^{\sigma,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}\leq\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\big\|_{W^{\sigma,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}+\big\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\big\|_{W^{\sigma,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}\lesssim 1.

Thus (
4.10
) follows from interpolating (
4.2
) and (
4.13
).

We can then write

\displaystyle(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}})G^{1-\Delta}(x,y)-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})G^{1-\Delta}(x,y)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}-\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})\big)(G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta})(x,y).

As for the terms on the first line, we estimate them with the use of (
4.4
), which is of course enough for (
4.5
). For the terms on the last line, since by Lemma
4.3
,
G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta}\in L^{\infty}W^{\sigma,p}
for
4\leq p<\infty
and
\frac{2}{p}<\sigma<\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1}{2}
, we have together with Sobolev embedding and (
4.10
):

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\otimes(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}-\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})\big)(G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\big(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\otimes(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}-\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})\big)(G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}W^{\frac{2}{p}+\varepsilon,p}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}\big\|G^{\mathcal{H}}-G^{1-\Delta}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}W^{\frac{2}{p}+\varepsilon+\delta,p}_{y}}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}.

The difference
(\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})G^{\mathcal{H}}-(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}})G^{\mathcal{H}}
is estimated similarly. This finally proves (
4.5
) provided that we take
\varepsilon,\delta>0
with
\varepsilon+\delta<\frac{1}{2}
.
∎

We have similar estimates if we use a “regularization” based on
\mathcal{H}
instead of
\Delta
.

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
be as in (
1.6
).

(i)
There exists
C>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
(x,y)\in\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathrm{diag}
it holds

(4.14)

\displaystyle\Big|(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H}))G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(\mathbf{d}(x,y)+N^{-1}\big)\Big|\leq C.

(ii)
For all
0<\delta\ll 1
there exists
c,C>0
such that for any
1\leq M_{1}\leq M_{2}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, it holds for any
j\in\{1;2\}
:

\displaystyle\Big\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{j}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{j}^{-2}\mathcal{H}))G^{\mathcal{H}}-(\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{2}^{-2}\mathcal{H}))G^{\mathcal{H}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq CN^{c\delta}M_{1}^{-\delta}.

Proof.

For (
4.14
), we decompose

\displaystyle(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H}))G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big((\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})\otimes\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}(x,y).

The first term is estimated by Corollary (
4.5
). As for the second term, we use the uniform boundedness of
\mathbf{P}_{N}
on
L^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
and Lemma
4.7
below to get for
0<\varepsilon,\kappa\ll 1

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\big(1\otimes(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}W^{\frac{2}{p}+\varepsilon,p}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}H^{\kappa+\varepsilon}_{y}}\lesssim 1.

Similarly, we have by symmetry and Lemma
4.7
again that

\displaystyle\big\|\big((\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})\otimes\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\big\|\big(1\otimes(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})^{2}\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}W^{\frac{2}{p}+\varepsilon,p}_{y}}+\big\|\big(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\big\|\big(1\otimes(\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\mathbf{P}_{N})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}H^{\kappa+\varepsilon}_{y}}+1

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim 1.

This shows (
4.14
).

As for (
4.6
), we start by showing the analogue of (
4.10
). From (
4.11
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\psi(M_{2}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big\|_{W^{\sigma+2+\varepsilon,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(M_{1}^{-2}-M_{2}^{-2})\int_{0}^{1}\big\|\mathcal{H}\psi^{\prime}\big((\theta M_{1}^{-1}+(1-\theta)M_{2}^{-2})\mathcal{H}\big)\big\|_{W^{\sigma+2+\varepsilon,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}d\theta

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(M_{1}^{-2}-M_{2}^{-2})\int_{0}^{1}\big\|\psi^{\prime}\big((\theta M_{1}^{-1}+(1-\theta)M_{2}^{-2})\mathcal{H}\big)\big\|_{W^{\sigma+2+\varepsilon,p}\to W^{\sigma+2,p}}d\theta

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim M_{1}^{-2},

thanks to Lemma
4.7
. Interpolating with

\displaystyle\big\|\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\psi(M_{2}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big\|_{W^{\sigma+\varepsilon,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}\lesssim 1

due to Lemma
4.7
again, we get

(4.16)

\displaystyle\big\|\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\psi(M_{2}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big\|_{W^{\sigma+\delta+\varepsilon,p}\to W^{\sigma,p}}\lesssim M_{1}^{-\delta},

for any
\delta\in[0;2]
.

Then we estimate using Lemma
A.5
and (
4.16
):

\displaystyle\Big\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H}))G^{\mathcal{H}}-(\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N}\psi(M_{2}^{-2}\mathcal{H}))G^{\mathcal{H}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|(\mathbf{P}_{N}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{N})\big(\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes(\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\psi(M_{2}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\Big\|_{W^{\frac{2}{p}+\varepsilon,p}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-2(\frac{2}{p}+2\varepsilon)}\Big\|\big(\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\otimes(\psi(M_{1}^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\psi(M_{2}^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big)G^{\mathcal{H}}\Big\|_{W^{-\varepsilon,p}_{x,y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{2(\frac{2}{p}+2\varepsilon)}M_{1}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}\big\|G^{\mathcal{H}}\big\|_{W^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2},p}_{x}W^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4},p}_{y}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{2(\frac{2}{p}+2\varepsilon)}M_{1}^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}}.

This shows (
4.6
) provided that we take
p
large enough and
\varepsilon
small enough.
∎

4.3.
Comparison of semi-classical multipliers

In this part, we establish a comparison principle between Schwartz multipliers for the Anderson operator and Schwartz multipliers for the Laplace-Beltrami operator. This is similar to (A.15) in
[
11
]
, but there the authors deal with abstract symmetric perturbations of
-\Delta
of order 1 in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, whereas here we deal with a perturbation of order
1+\kappa
,
0<\kappa\ll 1
, with rough coefficients.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
,
2\leq p<\infty
and
\frac{2}{p}-1<\sigma<\frac{2}{p}+1
, and
0<\kappa<\delta<1+\kappa
and
\kappa-\delta\leq\beta\leq 2+\kappa-\delta
such that
0\leq\sigma-\frac{2}{p}+\delta\leq 2
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any
N\geq 1
and
f\in H^{\beta}(\mathcal{M})
, it holds

(4.17)

\displaystyle\big\|\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})f-\psi(N^{-2}\Delta)f\big\|_{W^{\sigma,p}}\leq CN^{\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\|f\|_{H^{\beta}}.

Moreover, if
\psi
is compactly supported away from 0, it holds

(4.18)

\displaystyle\big\|\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})f-\psi(N^{-2}\Delta)f\big\|_{W^{\sigma,p}}\leq CN^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta}\|f\|_{H^{\beta}}.

Proof.

The proof closely follows that of
[
11
, Proposition 2.1 and Theorem 6]
: by Helffer-Sjöstrand’s formula, we have

(4.19)

\displaystyle\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\psi(-N^{-2}\Delta)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{\partial}\widetilde{\psi}(z)\Big((z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H})^{-1}-(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}\Big)dz,

where
\widetilde{\psi}
is an almost analytic extension of
\psi
. Here we take

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}(z)=\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{2}\frac{\psi^{(k)}(\Re z)}{k!}(i\Im z)^{k}\Big)\chi(\Im z)

with
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying
\chi\equiv 1
near 0, so that

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}\widetilde{\psi}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\widetilde{\psi}^{(2)}(\Re z)}{2}(i\Im z)^{2}\chi(\Im z)+\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\psi}^{(k)}(\Re z)}{k!}(i\Im z)^{k}\Big)\chi^{\prime}(\Im z),

providing

(4.20)

|\overline{\partial}\widetilde{\psi}(z)|\lesssim\langle\Re z\rangle^{-10}|\Im z|^{2}\mathbf{1}_{|\Im z|\lesssim 1}.

Then we decompose the right-hand side of (
4.19
) as

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{\partial}\widetilde{\psi}(z)\Big((z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}-(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}\Big)dz\Gamma^{-1}

(4.21)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{\pi}(\Gamma-1)\int_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{\partial}\widetilde{\psi}(z)(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}dz\Gamma^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{\partial}\widetilde{\psi}(z)(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}dz(\Gamma^{-1}-1).

We start by estimating the norm of the semi-classical resolvents in Sobolev spaces. First, for any
\nu\in(-1;2]
, since
\mathcal{D}^{\nu}=\Gamma H^{\nu}
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu}}

\displaystyle\sim\big\|(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H})^{-1}\Gamma u\big\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu}}\sim\Big(\sum_{n\geq 0}\lambda_{n}^{\nu}\frac{|\langle\Gamma u,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}}{|z+N^{-2}\lambda_{n}|^{2}}\Big)^{2}

(4.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Im z|^{-1}\|\Gamma u\|_{D^{\nu}},

and similarly, for any
\nu\in\mathbb{R}
,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\big\|(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu}}\lesssim|\Im z|^{-1}\|u\|_{H^{\nu}},

uniformly in
N
. Moreover, if
u\in H^{\nu-2}(\mathcal{M})
, letting
v=(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}u
, we have
(1-\Delta)v=v+N^{2}(zv-u)
, from which we infer

\displaystyle\big\|(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+N^{2}|z|)\big\|(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu-2}}+N^{2}\|u\|_{H^{\nu-2}}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Im z|^{-1}\langle z\rangle N^{2}\|u\|_{H^{\nu-2}}.

Proceeding similarly, since
v=(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H})^{-1}\Gamma u
satisfies
\mathcal{H}v=zN^{2}v-N^{2}\Gamma u
, we have for any
\nu\in(-1;2]
:

\displaystyle\big\|(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu}}

\displaystyle\sim\big\|(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H})^{-1}\Gamma u\big\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu}}

(4.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim|z|N^{2}\big\|(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H})^{-1}\Gamma u\big\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu-2}}+N^{2}\|\Gamma u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Im z|^{-1}\langle z\rangle N^{2}\|\Gamma u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu-2}},

where we used that
\Gamma
is invertible on
H^{s}
,
s\in(-1;1)
and
\Gamma H^{s}=\mathcal{D}^{s}
for
s\in(-1;2]
.

Interpolation between (
4.3
) and (
4.3
) on the one hand, and between (
4.23
) and (
4.3
) on the other hand, leads to both

(4.26)

\displaystyle\big\|(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu}}\lesssim|\Im z|^{-1}\langle z\rangle^{\frac{\eta}{2}}N^{\eta}\|\Gamma u\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\nu-\eta}}

for any
\nu\in(-1;2]
and
\eta\in[0;2]
, and

(4.27)

\displaystyle\big\|(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}u\big\|_{H^{\nu}}\lesssim|\Im z|^{-1}\langle z\rangle^{\frac{\eta}{2}}N^{\eta}\|u\|_{H^{\nu-\eta}},

for any
\nu\in\mathbb{R}
and
\eta\in[0;2]
.

We can then estimate the terms in the right-hand side of (
4.21
). For the first term, we use the resolvent identity, (
4.20
), (
4.26
)-(
4.27
), and the mapping property of
G_{\xi}
and
\mathcal{H}\Gamma
, to get

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{C}}\bar{\partial}\widetilde{\psi}(z)\Big((z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}-(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}\Big)dz\Gamma^{-1}f\Big\|_{W^{\sigma,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|\Im z|\lesssim 1}\langle z\rangle^{-10}|\Im z|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\big\|(z-N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}N^{-2}(G_{\xi}+(\mathcal{H}\Gamma\cdot)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma-\frac{2}{p}+\delta-2}\int_{|\Im z|\lesssim 1}\langle z\rangle^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{\delta}{2}-10}|\Im z|\Big\{\big\|G_{\xi}(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}\Gamma^{-1}f\big\|_{H^{1-\delta}}dz

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}X\Big\|_{H^{1-\delta}}\Big\}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma-\frac{2}{p}+\delta-2}\int_{|\Im z|\lesssim 1}\langle z\rangle^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{\delta}{2}-10}|\Im z|\Big\{\big\|(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}\Gamma^{-1}f\|_{H^{2-\delta+\kappa}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big\|\mathcal{H}\Gamma(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}\Gamma^{-1}f\big\|_{H^{\kappa-\delta}}\Big\}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma-\frac{2}{p}+\delta-2}\int_{|\Im z|\lesssim 1}\langle z\rangle^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{\delta}{2}-10}|\Im z|\big\|(z+N^{-2}\Delta)^{-1}\Gamma^{-1}f\big\|_{H^{2-\delta+\kappa}}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma-\frac{2}{p}+\kappa-\beta}\int_{|\Im z|\lesssim 1}\langle z\rangle^{\frac{\sigma+\kappa-\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{p}-10}\big\|\Gamma^{-1}f\big\|_{H^{\beta}}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma-\frac{2}{p}+\kappa-\beta}\|f\|_{H^{\beta}},

where in the last step we used that
\Gamma^{-1}
is bounded on
H^{\beta}(\mathcal{M})
by choice of
\beta
.

As for the second term in (
4.21
), we write again
\Gamma=1+B
as in (
4.5
), with
B
bounded from
W^{\sigma-1+\kappa,p}(\mathcal{M})
to
W^{\sigma,p}(\mathcal{M})
. Using that
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
, this term contributes as

\displaystyle\Big\|B\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H}^{\sharp})^{-1}\Gamma^{-1}f\Big\|_{W^{\sigma,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\Gamma^{-1}\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})f\big\|_{W^{\sigma-1+\kappa,p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\Gamma^{-1}\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})f\big\|_{H^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}}}\sim\big\|\psi(N^{-2}\mathcal{H})f\big\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{n\geq 0}\psi(N^{-2}\lambda_{n})\lambda_{n}^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}}|\langle f,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{L}\Big(\sum_{n\geq 0}\langle N^{-2}\lambda_{n}\rangle^{-L}\lambda_{n}^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}}|\langle f,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum_{\lambda_{n}\lesssim N^{2}}\lambda_{n}^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}}|\langle f,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Big(\sum_{\lambda_{n}\gg N^{2}}N^{2L}\lambda_{n}^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-L}|\langle f,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\sum_{\lambda_{n}\lesssim N^{2}}N^{2\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\lambda_{n}^{\beta}|\langle f,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Big(\sum_{\lambda_{n}\gg N^{2}}N^{2\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\lambda_{n}^{\beta}|\langle f,\varphi_{n}\rangle|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}^{\beta}}\sim N^{\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\|f\|_{H^{\beta}}

since
L\geq 0
is arbitrary, and since
\mathcal{D}^{\beta}=H^{\beta}
for the range of
\beta
in Lemma
4.7
.

A.8
), the third term in (

) is estimated similarly by

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\big\|f\mathbin{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\|f\|_{B^{\beta+\frac{2}{p}-1}_{p,2}}\lesssim N^{\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}\|f\|_{H^{\beta}}.

Putting everything together finally proves (
4.17
).

As for (
4.18
), the only differences are that in treating the second term in the right-hand side of (
4.21
), the sums on
\lambda_{n}\lesssim N^{2}
and
\lambda_{n}\gg N^{2}
are now reduced to a single sum on
\lambda_{n}\sim N^{2}
, for which the same bound holds with
N^{\max(\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta,0)}
replaced by
N^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta}
. Similarly for the third term, using the definition of Besov spaces, that
\psi
is compactly supported away from zero, together with the definition (
A.2
) of the functional calculus, and (
A.7
):

\displaystyle\big\|\psi(N^{-2}\Delta)(f\mathbin{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
