\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}X)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x}\ell^{2}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta}\big\|(f\mathbin{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\textstyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{0.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}X)\big\|_{B^{\beta+\frac{2}{p}-\kappa}_{p,2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta}\big\|f\big\|_{B^{\beta+\frac{2}{p}-1}_{p,2}}\lesssim N^{\sigma+\kappa-\frac{2}{p}-\beta}\big\|f\big\|_{H^{\beta}}.

This proves (
4.18
).
∎

As a Corollary, we will use the following uniform boundedness property of smooth projectors for
\mathcal{H}
in Hölder spaces.

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
. Then for any
\sigma\in(-1;1)
and
0<\kappa\ll 1
it holds

\displaystyle\big\|\chi(M^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma+\kappa}\to\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim 1,

uniformly in
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.

Proof.

Applying Lemmas
4.7
and (
A.5
), we get for
p\gg 1
:

\displaystyle\big\|\chi(M^{-2}\mathcal{H})\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma+\kappa}\to\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\chi(M^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\chi(M^{-2}\Delta)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma+\kappa}\to\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}+\big\|\chi(M^{-2}\Delta)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma+\kappa}\to\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\chi(M^{-2}\mathcal{H})-\chi(M^{-2}\Delta)\big\|_{H^{\sigma+\kappa}\to W^{\sigma+\frac{2}{p}+\frac{\kappa}{2},p}}+M^{-\kappa}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+M^{-\kappa}.

∎

Appendix A
Analytic toolbox

A.1.
Analysis on manifolds and function spaces

Recall that we work on a two-dimensional closed (compact, boundaryless), connected, orientable smooth Riemannian manifold
(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{\mathrm{g}})
, where we fix the metric
\mathbf{\mathrm{g}}
once and for all and drop it in the remaining of the paper. We set
\{\varphi_{n}^{\Delta}\}_{n\geq 0}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
to be a basis of
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
consisting of eigenfunctions of
-\Delta
associated with the eigenvalue
\lambda_{n}^{\Delta}\geq 0
, assumed to be arranged in increasing order:
0\leq\lambda_{0}^{\Delta}<\lambda_{1}^{\Delta}\leq\lambda_{2}^{\Delta}\leq...
As for
\lambda_{n}^{\Delta}
,
n\to\infty
, we have the following asymptotic behaviour given by Weyl’s law:

(A.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{n}^{\Delta}}{n}\longrightarrow\frac{|\mathcal{M}|}{4\pi}\text{ as }n\to\infty.

Using the above basis of
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
, we can expand any
u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathcal{M})
as

\displaystyle u=\sum_{n\geq 0}\langle u,\varphi_{n}^{\Delta}\rangle_{\mathbf{\mathrm{g}}}\varphi_{n}^{\Delta},

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathbf{\mathrm{g}}}
denotes the distributional pairing
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{M})\to\mathbb{R}
which coincides with the usual inner product in
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
for distributions which are regular enough. For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
1\leq p\leq\infty
, we thus define the Sobolev spaces

\displaystyle W^{s,p}(\mathcal{M},\mathbf{\mathrm{g}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\Big\{u\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathcal{M}),~\|u\|_{W^{s,p}(\mathcal{M})}<\infty\Big\}

where

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W^{s,p}(\mathcal{M})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\big\|(1-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}u\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{M})}=\Big\|\sum_{n\geq 0}(1+\lambda_{n}^{\Delta})^{s}\langle u,\varphi_{n}^{\Delta}\rangle_{\mathbf{\mathrm{g}}}\varphi_{n}^{\Delta}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{M})}.

When
p=2
we write
H^{s}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}W^{s,2}(\mathcal{M})
.

Next, recall that for any self-adjoint elliptic operator
\mathbf{A}
on
L^{2}(\mathcal{M})
with discrete spectrum
\{\lambda_{n}\}
and orthonormal basis of eigenfunctions
\{\varphi_{n}\}
, the functional calculus of
\mathbf{A}
is defined for any
\psi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
by

(A.2)

\displaystyle\psi\big(\mathbf{A}\big)u=\sum_{n\geq 0}\psi(\lambda_{n}^{\mathbf{A}})\langle u,\varphi_{n}^{\mathbf{A}}\rangle\varphi_{n}^{\mathbf{A}},

for all
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
. This in particular allows us to define the more general class of Besov spaces on
\mathcal{M}
associated with
\Delta
. First, using the functional calculus, we can define the Littlewood-Paley projectors
\mathbf{Q}_{M}
for a dyadic integer
M\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\{0,1,2,4,...\}
as

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{M}=\begin{cases}\psi_{0}\big(-(2M)^{-2}\Delta\big)-\psi_{0}\big(-M^{-2}\Delta\big),~~M\geq 1,\\
\psi_{0}\big(-\Delta\big),~~M=0,\end{cases}

where
\psi_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
is non-negative and such that
\supp\psi_{0}\subset[-1,1]
and
\psi_{0}\equiv 1
on
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]
. With the inhomogeneous dyadic partition of unity
\{\mathbf{Q}_{M}\}_{M\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}_{-1}}}
, we can then define the Besov norms for
p,q\in[1,\infty]
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\Big\|\langle M\rangle^{qs}\|\mathbf{Q}_{M}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{M})}\Big\|_{\ell^{q}_{M}}=\Big(\sum_{M\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}_{-1}}}\langle M\rangle^{qs}\big\|\mathbf{Q}_{M}f\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{M})}^{q}\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

for any smooth function
f
. The last sum is of course understood to be the supremum for
q=\infty
.

Definition A.1
.

Fix
p,q\in[1,\infty]
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. We define the Besov space
\mathcal{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})
to be the completion of
C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
for the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})}
. In particular, this defines the Hölder spaces
\mathcal{C}^{s}(\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathcal{M})
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

These function spaces are the natural generalization of the usual Besov spaces on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to the context of closed manifolds. Let us recall the following characterization of these spaces from
[
39
, Proposition 2.5]

Lemma A.2
.

Let
(U,V,\kappa)
be a coordinate patch and
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(V)
. For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
1\leq p,r\leq\infty
, there exist
c,C>0
such that for any
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
,

\displaystyle c\|\chi u\|_{B^{s}_{p,r}(\mathcal{M})}\leq\|\kappa^{\star}(\chi u)\|_{B^{s}_{p,r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p,r}(\mathcal{M})}.

This allows to transfer the usual linear or nonlinear estimates on Besov spaces on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to the case of Besov spaces on
\mathcal{M}
; see for example Lemma
A.3
below, which is taken from
[
39
, Corollary 2.7]
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})
be the Besov spaces defined above. Then the following properties hold.

(i)
For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
we have
B^{s}_{2,2}(\mathcal{M})=H^{s}(\mathcal{M})
, and more generally for any
2\leq p<\infty
and
\varepsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim\|u\|_{W^{s,p}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p,2}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim\|u\|_{B^{s+\varepsilon}_{p,\infty}(\mathcal{M})}.

(ii)
Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
1\leq p_{1}\leq p_{2}\leq\infty
and
q_{1},q_{2}\in[1,\infty]
. Then for any
f\in B^{s}_{p_{1},q}(\mathcal{M})
we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B^{s-2\big(\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{2}}\big)}_{p_{2},q}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p_{1},q}(\mathcal{M})}.

(iii)
Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
with
\alpha+\beta>0
, and
p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in[1,\infty]
with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}.

Then for any
f\in B^{\alpha}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathcal{M})
and
g\in B^{\beta}_{p_{2},q_{2}}(\mathcal{M})
, we have
fg\in B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})
, and it holds:

•

If
\alpha\wedge\beta<0
, then

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathcal{M})}\|g\|_{B^{\beta}_{p_{2},q_{2}}(\mathcal{M})}.

•

If
\alpha\wedge\beta>0
, then

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p_{1},q_{1}}(\mathcal{M})}\|g\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p_{2},q_{2}}(\mathcal{M})}.

We will also make use of the following fractional Leibniz rule.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
r,s<1
with
r+s>0
, and
t=r+s-1
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any
u\in H^{s}(\mathcal{M})
and
v\in H^{r}(\mathcal{M})
, we have the fractional Leibniz rule

\|uv\|_{H^{t}}\leq C\|u\|_{H^{s}}\|v\|_{H^{r}}.

Proof.

The corresponding estimate in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is proved in
[
51
, Corollary 2.1]
. Then the one on
\mathcal{M}
follows from the one in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
through the use of a finite partition of unity and Lemma
A.2
; see the proof of Lemma
A.3
(iii) in
[
39
]
for details.
∎

A.2.
Schwartz multipliers of the Laplace-Beltrami operator

We will need to estimate the action of semi-classical multipliers on
\mathcal{M}
with symbol in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
. We start by recalling the following universal bound on their kernel from
[
40
, Lemma 2.5]
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
, and for any
h\in(0,1]
define the kernel

(A.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{h}(x,y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{n\geq 0}\psi\big(h^{2}\lambda_{n}^{\Delta}\big)\varphi_{n}(x)\varphi_{n}(y)

Then for any
L\geq 0
, there exists
C>0
such that for any
h\in(0,1]
and
x,y\in\mathcal{M}
it holds

(A.6)

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{K}_{h}(x,y)\big|\leq Ch^{-2}\langle h^{-1}\mathbf{d}(x,y)\rangle^{-L},

where
\mathbf{d}
is the geodesic distance on
\mathcal{M}
. In particular, it holds

(A.7)

\displaystyle\Big\|\psi(-h^{2}\Delta)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{M})\to L^{q}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim h^{-2(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})}

and

(A.8)

\displaystyle\Big\|\psi(-h^{2}\Delta)\Big\|_{B^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})\to B^{\sigma_{1}}_{p,q}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim h^{-\sigma_{2}}

for any
h\in(0,1]
and
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and
\sigma_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\sigma_{2}\geq 0
.

Proof.

Estimates (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) are proved in
[
40
, Lemma 2.5]
. As for (
A.8
), it is a straightforward adaptation of the proof of (
A.11
) below given in
[
40
, Lemma 2.6]
, since there only the fact that
e^{t\Delta}
is a Schwartz multiplier was used, but not the precise form of its symbol; see (2.13) in
[
40
]
.
∎

As a corollary of the estimates above, we can then investigate the behaviour of the solutions to the linear heat equation on
\mathcal{M}
. Indeed, we can use the eigenfunctions expansion to represent the solution of the heat equation

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u-\Delta u=0,\\
u(0)=u_{0}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathcal{M})\end{cases}~~(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{M},

as the distribution

\displaystyle u(t)=e^{t\Delta}u_{0}=\sum_{n\geq 0}e^{-t\lambda_{n}^{\Delta}}\langle u_{0},\varphi_{n}\rangle\varphi_{n}.

The well-known heat kernel is then the kernel of the above propagator, defined as

(A.9)

\displaystyle p_{t}^{\Delta}(x,y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{n\geq 0}e^{-t\lambda_{n}^{\Delta}}\varphi_{n}(x)\varphi_{n}(y).

As a corollary to Lemma
A.5
, we have the following bounds; see
[
40
, Lemma 2.6]
for a proof.

Lemma A.6
.

(i)
For any
L\geq 0
, there exists
C>0
such that for any
0<t\leq 1
and
x,y\in\mathcal{M}
it holds

(A.10)

\displaystyle|p_{t}^{\Delta}(x,y)|\leq Ct^{-1}\langle t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{d}(x,y)\rangle^{-L}.

(ii)
For any
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, any
1\leq r\leq\infty
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\alpha_{1}\leq\alpha_{2}
, there exists
C>0
such that for any
0<t\leq 1
and any
u\in B^{\alpha_{1}}_{p,r}(\mathcal{M})
, we have
Schauder’s estimate

(A.11)

\displaystyle\big\|e^{t\Delta}u\big\|_{B^{\alpha_{2}}_{q,r}(\mathcal{M})}\leq Ct^{-\frac{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}}{2}-(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})}\big\|u\big\|_{B^{\alpha_{1}}_{p,r}(\mathcal{M})}.

We will also use of the following bound on the kernel of fractional antiderivatives on
\mathcal{M}
.

Lemma A.7
.

For
\sigma\in(0;2)
, let
G_{\sigma}
be the distributional kernel of
(1-\Delta)^{-\frac{\sigma}{2}}
. Then
G_{\sigma}
is non-negative distribution, smooth away from the diagonal in
\mathcal{M}^{2}
, and we have the estimate

(A.12)

\displaystyle G_{\sigma}(x,y)\lesssim\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{\sigma-2}.

Proof.

Using the eigenfunction expansion, we can write

\displaystyle G_{\sigma}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{\varphi_{n}(x)\varphi_{n}(y)}{(1+\lambda_{n}^{\Delta})^{\sigma}}=\sum_{n\geq 0}\varphi_{n}(x)\varphi_{n}(y)\Gamma\Big(\frac{\sigma}{2}\Big)^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-1}e^{-t(1+\lambda_{n}^{\Delta})}dt

\displaystyle=\Gamma\Big(\frac{\sigma}{2}\Big)^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-1}e^{-t}p_{t}^{\Delta}(x,y)dt,

where
\Gamma
is the Gamma function, and the equality holds in the sense of distributions on
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}
. Note that the last integral converges since we assumed
\sigma>0
. This shows that
G_{\sigma}
is a non-negative distribution, smooth away from the diagonal, and from the estimate (
A.10
) on the heat kernel, we get for
0<\mathbf{d}(x,y)\leq 1
:

\displaystyle G_{\sigma}(x,y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}t^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-2}\langle t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{d}(x,y)\rangle^{-10}dt+1

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{2}}t^{\frac{\sigma}{2}+3}\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{-10}+\int_{\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{2}}^{1}t^{\frac{\sigma}{2}-2}+1

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbf{d}(x,y)^{\sigma-2}.

This shows (
A.12
).
∎
